## 42. SPARK: Simple Post-training for Adapting pRetrained Knowledge to Robot Control

- **作者**：Taekyung Kim, Jeongeun Park, Byeongho Heo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：CoRL 2026. 16 pages, 3 figures, 13 tables

Large-scale representation learning has produced public foundation models that provide strong general-purpose visual features. However, their pretraining objectives are not designed for robot representation learning, so the resulting embeddings tend to emphasize global semantics rather than the temporally sensitive representations required for robot control. Training robot models from scratch is also undesirable due to the limited scale and high collection cost of robotics data, as well as the substantial computational burden of pre-training. In this paper, we propose SPARK (Simple Post-training for Adapting pRetrained Knowledge), a post-training method that adapts foundation models to robot control. SPARK is built on two principles: temporal abstraction, which encourages the encoder to derive compact features that preserve information essential for understanding temporal changes and action-relevant scene structure, and knowledge preservation, which aligns patch-level representations with those of the original foundation model to retain useful pretrained semantics. These objectives yield compact visual state representations that remain semantically meaningful while preserving useful prior knowledge. Experiments across multiple robotics benchmarks show that SPARK consistently improves success rates and generalization over vanilla foundation models, and further demonstrate that these gains transfer to real-world robot manipulation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12102) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12102)

## 43. Compass 1: Towards Open-World and Persistent Embodied Navigation from Monocular RGB Videos

- **作者**：Hai Zhang, Siqi Liang, Yanjun Guo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：Technical Report. Project Page: https://www.archon.tech/research/compass1

Embodied navigation requires agents to achieve persistent spatial awareness and long-horizon execution stability in open-world environments. However, existing navigation architectures struggle to sustain both capabilities, remaining effective only over a handful of recent frames. We present Compass 1, the first embodied navigation foundation model to unify language understanding and video generation while enabling memory backtracking through nearly 2mins of visual history. Without altering the underlying architecture, we introduce History Representation, which integrates sparse visual keyframes with an explicit History Representation Map to preserve long-term spatiotemporal context. To effectively leverage this history, we further introduce History Utilization, a structured VQA supervision protocol comprising three categories: task-stage grounding, exploration consistency, and spatiotemporal target retrieval, enabling map-grounded reasoning and stable persistent long-horizon navigation. To fuel this system, we establish a complete annotation pipeline for monocular RGB videos and open-source a massive dataset featuring over 500 hours of high-quality real-world interactions and over 2,000 hours of annotated web videos. Trained mainly on the distribution of 1m height camera, Compass 1 generalizes seamlessly across diverse embodiments and scenarios, ranging from indoor environments to pedestrian-dense streets, spanning wheeled robot at 0.35m, quadruped dog at 0.5m, humanoid at 0.85m, and aerial drone at 1.5m. Compass 1achieves a dominant overall success rate of 79.4% across open-world evaluations, compared to 17.8% for the strongest baseline. It demonstrates exceptional zero-shot execution exceeding 90% on foundational tasks, a 250% boost in exploration, and an unparalleled leap from 0% to 58% in challenging persistent history backtracking.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12087) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12087)

## 44. How a Model's Architecture and Performance Influences Explainability: A Study of Grad-CAM and CRAFT in CNN-Based Image Classification

- **作者**：Kilian Göller, Haadia Amjad, Steffen Seitz et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Accepted at the 4th World Conference on eXplainable Artificial Intelligence (xAI 2026), Fortaleza, Brazil, July 1-3, 2026. To appear in Springer CCIS. 12 pages, 5 figures

Deep learning models, particularly convolutional neural networks (CNNs), are widely adopted in computer vision, yet their opaque decision-making raises concerns in high-risk applications like healthcare and autonomous systems, where trust and accountability are critical. Therefore, robust explainable AI (XAI) methods are desirable to provide transparent insights into model decisions. To ensure these methods are reliable and effective across diverse scenarios, it is important to understand how model performance and architectural complexity influence explanation quality. Our study takes an initial step toward systematically investigating the impact of these two factors on key XAI evaluation metrics. We constructed a pipeline using 10 subsets of ImageNet-1K, each containing 80 unique classes, on each of which we trained three CNN architectures of increasing structural complexity: VGG16, ResNet50, and ConvNeXt-Tiny. For each individual model, we analyzed 10 training checkpoints representing increasing levels of model accuracy using two XAI methods: Grad-CAM (feature-attribution-based) and CRAFT (concept-based). We assessed these methods across four evaluation categories: Faithfulness, Correctness, Sensitivity, and Simplicity. Our findings reveal that as training progresses, the complex architectures (ResNet50 and ConvNeXt-Tiny) exhibit improved Faithfulness and lower Sensitivity, indicating more robust explanations. In contrast, VGG16's simpler architecture yields simpler but less faithful and correct explanations.

## 45. Color wave: A systematic evaluation of active screen illumination for injection attack detection in remote face authentication

- **作者**：Daniyar Kurmankhojayev, Elmurat Nigmatov, Dmitrii Gordin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Remote face authentication requires protection against both presentation attacks and biometric data injection attacks prior to face matching. Methods based on active screen illumination appear promising for both by introducing randomized visual stimuli through the user's device screen and evaluating the corresponding facial responses. Although such methods have been more studied for presentation attack detection (PAD), their systematic evaluation for injection attack detection (IAD) remains limited. To address this gap, we first classify injected content into static images, prerecorded videos, and real-time deepfakes based on their expected responses to the challenge. We then adapt an active screen-illumination approach into an implementation we term color wave and develop a systematic, injection-specific evaluation protocol. The protocol examines detection effectiveness, generalization across operating conditions, robustness to signal degradation, and susceptibility to selected evasion strategies. The findings demonstrate a strong discriminative signal under controlled conditions but also reveal performance limitations under overexposure, low screen brightness, and subject motion, as well as vulnerabilities to selected evasion strategies. By relating detection outcomes to attack types and operating conditions, this study provides an interpretable assessment of when and why active screen illumination succeeds or fails as an IAD mechanism.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12070) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12070)

## 46. MPEG-Diffusion: Mixed-Resolution Video Generation

- **作者**：Dennis Menn, Tianpei Gu, Guande He et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video diffusion transformers (DiTs) generate realistic videos, but their heavy computational cost limits broader real-world applications. Mixed-resolution generation reduces this cost by generating the visually important region at high resolution (HR) and the rest at low resolution (LR), yet existing methods suffer a severe quality drop on videos and require knowing the HR region in advance. We show that these failures are not inherent to mixed resolution, and propose $\textbf{MPEG-Diffusion}$ to address their three root causes: (1) dual-resolution attention to mitigate the curse of mixed resolution, where each query reads the K/V of its own resolution, (2) a mixed-resolution decoder to blend the HR and LR latents before decoding and resolve distortion, and (3) a self-selected HR region using the DiT's knowledge to find the visually important region without requiring it in advance. Processing only $30\%$ of full-resolution tokens, MPEG-Diffusion speeds up denoising by $4.8\times$ on Wan2.1 14B ($3.8\times$ on 1.3B), outperforming prior methods on nearly every metric. In the user study, we demonstrate the high quality of MPEG-Diffusion, with raters preferring it over prior mixed-resolution methods in over $90\%$ of pairs and rating it close to full resolution.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12069) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12069)

## 47. Weight-Space Programming for Medical Imaging: Do We Still Need Training Data?

- **作者**：Maximilian Lindholz, Richard Ruppel, Annette Milnik et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Can a large language model (LLM) write the parameters of a medical image classifier from medical knowledge alone, without any training data? Such classifiers normally require labelled images, which are costly and often unavailable. We investigate this question through weight-space programming (WSP), in which an LLM writes or adapts the weights and biases of a neural network directly, without optimisation on training data. Claude Opus 5.5 and GPT-6 Astra each generate the parameters of a small LeNet-style CNN in a single reply, without tool use and without access to any image. We compare these networks against zero-shot MedSigLIP and against the same architecture trained on labels using the Adam optimiser, across 7 classification tasks in four modalities: chest X-ray, breast ultrasound and abdominal CT (all from MedMNIST), and pelvic MRI (SynthUterus). The LLM-written networks perform above chance on all six MedMNIST tasks, with a mean test AUC of 0.719 for Opus and 0.711 for Astra. Both models outperform MedSigLIP on 5 of 6 tasks while using more than 10,000 times fewer parameters. However, the same network trained with Adam on only 256 labelled images achieves a higher AUC on every MedMNIST task (mean AUC 0.828). On SynthUterus, Opus attains an AUC of 0.764, whereas Astra falls below chance (AUC 0.136) owing to a systematic error. These results show that LLMs can write functional medical classifiers, but that they do not yet replace training data. We release the protocol, logs and all generated weights at https://github.com/MaximilianLindholz/weight-space-programming-medical.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12066) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12066)

## 48. Attention-Guided Sequence Feature Fusion and Mixed-Space Metric Learning for Cross-View Image Sequence Geo-Localization

- **作者**：Nan Wu, Chunfang Yang, Jiangshan Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Cross-view image sequence geo-localization aims to determine the geographic location of a sequence of ground images by matching it against geo-tagged satellite imagery. Although sequential observations provide complementary scene information, substantial overlap between adjacent images introduces feature redundancy, while conventional fusion strategies may weaken spatial relationships essential for cross-view matching. Therefore, we propose a cross-view image sequence geo-localization method via Attention-guided Sequence fusion and Metric Learning (ASML). Specifically, a Sequential Content Fusion (SCF) module models cross-image feature correlations to suppress redundant content and adaptively preserve geographically informative non-overlapping regions. Next, a Spatial Feature Aggregation (SFA) module is used to capture and aggregate the spatial and semantic information of local features, so as to maintain the spatial layout of the image sequence. Finally, a Geo-InfoNCE loss function is proposed, which comprehensively considers the feature distance and geographic distance for mixed-space metric learning, thus improving the ability of the model to distinguish hard negative samples. A new cross-view image sequence dataset CrossRoad-66K is established to provide more complex scenarios. Extensive experiments and visualizations on the SeqGeo and CrossRoad-66K datasets demonstrate that compared to state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods, the proposed method ASML achieves an improvement of 3.37% and 2.10% at R@1, respectively.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12051) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12051)

## 49. Leveraging High-Activity Post-Therapeutic I-131 Scans for Deep Learning Reconstruction of Extremely Sparse Planar Images in Differentiated Thyroid Cancer

- **作者**：Boyang Pan, Hang Yang, Jianchen Pan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV, physics.med-ph
- **备注**：12 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables

Background: Diagnostic I-131 whole-body planar imaging in differentiated thyroid cancer (DTC) is count-limited by low administered activity, and quality worsens under rapid acquisition. Models trained on such data struggle because their targets lack photon statistics. Purpose: To test whether high-quality post-therapeutic (PT) I-131 scans can serve as superior training references for enhancing sparse diagnostic planar images, using transfer learning and input-image addition. Methods: In this single-center prospective study, 111 patients were enrolled in PT training (n = 19), diagnostic follow-up (FU) training (n = 58), and FU paired test (n = 34) cohorts. Five Restormer-based models were trained: FU_DLx2 on FU labels; PT_DLx2 transfer-learned on PT data; PT_DLx1+ and PT_DLx2+ with input-image addition; PT_DLx6 as a PT-only reference. Test-time processing used two-step denormalization and Poisson re-scaling to preserve noise statistics. Quality was assessed by PSNR, SSIM, and a blinded dual-reader evaluation of all 34 test-cohort patients. Results: PT_DLx2 achieved the highest PSNR (34.4 +/- 3.6 dB) and SSIM (0.81 +/- 0.07). PT_DLx1+ and PT_DLx2+ were comparable (33.8 and 33.3 dB; SSIM 0.80). FU_DLx2 ranked below the unprocessed fast scan (30.3 vs. 32.0 dB; 0.76 vs. 0.79). Subjectively, PT_DLx2+ scored highest in image quality (4.54 +/- 0.60) and diagnostic confidence (4.54 +/- 0.60), surpassing the reference standard (4.19 +/- 0.83 and 4.31 +/- 0.81; P = 0.005 and P = 0.008). FU_DLx2 and PT_DLx6 scored lowest. Conclusion: Count-rich PT scans provide effective training priors for reconstructing extremely sparse images. Input-image addition and cross-domain transfer learning from PT data outperformed direct training on count-limited diagnostic data, supporting 2x scan acceleration with quality matching or exceeding the unaccelerated standard scan for selected PT-derived configurations.

## 50. Four-Step Text-Bearing Image Generation via Distillation with Local Response Regularization and Teacher-Guided Training

- **作者**：Kairui Wen, Sheng Ye, Yong-Jin Liu
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Generating text-bearing images requires coherent scenes and accurate characters. Large text-to-image models improve both capabilities through repeated denoising, at substantial sampling cost. Few-step distillation reduces this cost but requires the student to organize scenes and refine glyphs within a short trajectory. We present a four-step distillation framework combining local response regularization with teacher-guided text training. Built on distribution matching, the framework distills the GLM-Image DiT while retaining its autoregressive visual prior, glyph encoder, and VAE. A path-length penalty promotes consistent local responses of the conditional denoising field. Progressive training combines synthetic strings with teacher-generated imagery to preserve character accuracy and scene-integrated typography. The student achieves GenEval Overall 0.84 and CVTG-2K word accuracy 0.5045, compared with 0.81 and 0.1690 for four-step TDM. A coefficient study favors $10^{-5}$, and Qwen-Image experiments show additional gains from response regularization and text supervision. Single-image inference takes 1.742 s, compared with 5.818 s for the teacher, yielding a $3.34\times$ speedup at the same 40 GB GPU memory requirement.

## 51. Leveraging Complementary Event Frame and Voxel Representations for RGB-Guided Multimodal Pre-training

- **作者**：Wentao Wu, Xiao Wang, Yi Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

In this paper, we propose CM3AE 2.0, a unified RGB Frame and Event-Voxel/-Frame pre-training framework. By jointly learning multimodal spatiotemporal representations and exploiting latent cross-modal dependencies through cross-modal generation, the framework enhances multimodal information interaction and collaborative modeling capabilities. Specifically, we first propose an Event-aware Spatiotemporal Masked Reconstruction (ESMR) strategy to learn unified multimodal spatiotemporal representations. It leverages event responses to guide masking toward dynamically salient regions. Through spatiotemporal masked reconstruction, it learns spatial structures and their temporal variations from continuous RGB-Event observations. In addition, it incorporates multimodal fusion reconstruction and cross-modal contrastive learning to facilitate the modeling of cross-modal complementary information and the learning of shared representations. Building upon this, we further propose a Hierarchical Cross-modal Generation (HCG) strategy to learn latent cross-modal dependencies through visual and language generation. At the visual level, Event-conditioned RGB generation is performed using a pre-trained diffusion model to capture visual associations between the two modalities. At the language level, video-level text description generation based on joint RGB-Event representations establishes connections between multimodal visual features and linguistic semantics, further enriching the learning of cross-modal dependencies. Extensive experiments on four diverse downstream tasks demonstrate that CM3AE 2.0 consistently improves performance, validating the effectiveness and generalization capability of the proposed pre-training framework.

## 52. DPGS-SLAM: Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM for Dynamic Visual Sensing

- **作者**：Yifan Luo, Takafumi Matsumaru, Vibekananda Dutta et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

This paper presents DPGS-SLAM (Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM) for dense mapping in environments containing dynamic objects. Dynamic observations can contaminate camera tracking and scene reconstruction in visual SLAM (simultaneous localization and mapping). Although 3DGS (3D Gaussian splatting) enables efficient dense mapping, existing 3DGS-SLAM systems may exhibit instability when dynamic objects violate their underlying static-scene assumption. Excluding dynamic observations can reduce their influence on tracking and mapping, but may leave occluded static backgrounds missing or weakly constrained. Our proposed DPGS-SLAM is a monocular RGB SLAM framework that supports provisional representations of insufficiently observed static backgrounds through asynchronous diffusion-based background completion and provenance-aware Gaussian mapping. Dynamic regions are identified through optical-flow-based motion discovery and temporally consistent mask propagation by SAM2 (segment anything model 2). For selected keyframes, a fine-tuned diffusion inpainting model generates plausible background appearance in occluded regions, while Metric3D v2 predicts metric depth from the completed images to initialize provisional Gaussian primitives. Provenance labels distinguish generated primitives from observation-supported primitives, excluding generated content from camera-pose estimation and observed-map optimization. When previously occluded backgrounds become visible, eligible generated primitives are removed, and observation-supported primitives are independently initialized from real RGB observations and predicted depth. Experimental results demonstrated competitive camera tracking, with average ATE RMSE values of 1.2 cm on TUM RGB-D and 2.7 cm on BONN RGB-D. On StaDy4D, DPGS-SLAM improved average PSNR by 4.60 dB over WildGS-SLAM using only monocular RGB input.

## 53. SAFE-Hair: Scalp-Anchored Fields for Exportable Single-View Hair Reconstruction

- **作者**：Yucheng Wang, Zedong Wang, Yuetong Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026

Single-view 3D hair reconstruction has progressed rapidly, but many methods optimize for visual plausibility rather than producing strand assets that can be directly used in grooming, editing, or simulation pipelines. We propose SAFE-Hair, a framework that reconstructs explicit strand grooms from a single portrait by decoding hair from persistent follicles in a canonical scalp-UV domain. Given a fitted template head, SAFE-Hair projects self-supervised image features onto the scalp, uses a conditional Rectified Flow model to generate latent scalp fields, and decodes occupancy, segment directions, length allocation, and total length at a fixed set of scalp follicles. This representation enforces scalp-rooted strand identities by construction and enables direct export with follicle IDs and guide-family metadata. We further introduce attachment, collision, and bending priors to improve geometric validity without per-subject test-time optimization. To evaluate both reconstruction fidelity and asset usability, we introduce HairBench-3D, a benchmark built from public 3D hair assets with standardized alignment, rendering, resampling, and metrics for geometry, direction consistency, invisible-region completion, silhouette agreement, and collision. SAFE-Hair improves Chamfer distance from 3.74 mm to 3.28 mm, F-score from 77.9 to 81.7, invisible-region F-score from 66.1 to 69.8, and penetration ratio from 4.8% to 3.9% over the strongest baseline.

## 54. Single Image Super-Resolution via Semantic Prompt and Cross-Modal Training on Content Area

- **作者**：Xiao Tan
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages, 7 figures, 5 tables

Enhancing image quality through super-resolution is crucial in image and video processing. While contemporary deep learning models, such as CNNs and Transformers, excel in pixel-level enhancements, they often overlook the rich semantic context within images. To address this, we introduce a novel approach that synergizes pixel intensity with semantic information for superior detail recovery. Our method involves extracting semantic data, including textual descriptors and spatial positioning, and encoding this information. We also present a Cross-Modal Feature Fusion module for generating semantic feature maps via multimodal training, and a Prompt Traction Head to augment the backbone network with semantic insights, thereby refining image details. Our empirical results across various datasets validate the superiority of our approach.

## 55. TIRAD: Text-guided Image Residual Learning for Generalist Anomaly Detection

- **作者**：Seongsu Lee, Seunghyun Oh, Seungjae Moon et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to ACCV 2026

Recent anomaly detection studies have shown that residual features, which model the discrepancy between normal and abnormal patterns, provide strong generalization to anomalies unseen during training. However, existing residual-based approaches mainly rely on visual discrepancies derived from image representations, while semantic discrepancies that can be explicitly expressed through language remain largely underexplored. To address this problem, we propose TIRAD, Text-guided Image Residual Learning for Generalist Anomaly Detection, a multimodal residual learning framework that defines the discrepancy between textual representations of normal and abnormal states as a Text Residual Feature and uses it to guide the Image Residual Feature. Specifically, a Text Fusion Module converts patch-level text residuals into a gate that modulates the image residual of each patch. The gate retains the visual discrepancies that the text residual identifies as defect-related and attenuates the others, leading to more discriminative anomaly representations. Extensive experiments on industrial benchmarks and industrial-to-medical transfer demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms existing image-centric residual learning methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12023) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12023)

## 56. HIST3R: Decomposing and Rectifying State Updates with History for Streaming 3D Reconstruction

- **作者**：Kenneth Yang, Yu-Chiang Frank Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://kennethyangg.github.io/HIST3R/

Streaming 3D reconstruction with recurrent states enables long-video scene understanding under bounded memory, but turns reconstruction into a state-evolution problem: each incoming frame updates a compact scene memory on which all subsequent predictions depend, so unreliable updates overwrite historical context and accumulate into long-horizon geometric drift. We analyze CUT3R-style recurrent updates through a direction--magnitude decomposition, in which the magnitude sets the update gain, i.e., how strongly an observation is written into the state, and the direction encodes the geometric intent, i.e., where the update drives the state. This decomposition provides a unified view of CUT3R and its variants, revealing that existing training-free extensions perform magnitude-only control: they can attenuate an unreliable update but cannot correct its direction. We propose HIST3R, a training-free framework built on CUT3R that controls both components using history. HIST3R rectifies the update direction using locally consistent historical states, and modulates the update magnitude according to the visual discrepancy between the current observation and its history, suppressing redundant updates while preserving complementary evidence. Across camera pose estimation, 3D reconstruction, and video depth estimation, HIST3R consistently improves long-horizon performance while retaining the memory footprint and inference speed of CUT3R. With 1000 input frames, it reduces ATE by 23.87% on ScanNet and 33.65% on TUM-Dynamics relative to the strongest competing method; with 500 frames, it improves median reconstruction accuracy over the strongest CUT3R-based variant by 56.52% on 7-Scenes and 33.96% on NRGBD.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12011) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.12011)

## 57. EmoHarness: Boosting Multimodal Emotion Understanding with Meta-Harness Evolving

- **作者**：Fenghai Li, Bingxuan Li, Jiawen Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, 11 figures, 6 tables. Project page: https://emo-harness.github.io. Code: https://github.com/Emo-Harness/EmoHarness

Small multimodal models can misjudge emotion when words, voice, and visual cues conflict. We present EmoHarness, which runs such a model, the frozen student, inside a harness: the prompts, settings, and verification code around its calls. A human-written starting harness, the scaffold, numbers the student's visual observations, transcript, and prosody descriptions as citable evidence and has the student weigh candidate emotions against it. In Meta-Harness Evolving (MHE), a stronger LLM, the Builder, reads the student's errors and execution traces offline and edits the harness; static checks and validation gates decide which edits are kept, and the Builder never sees evaluation items. On 5,507 MME-Emotion items, EmoHarness raises task-macro Recognition by 7.6-13.4 points over a single video-only call for all four students and both Builders. Transcripts and prosody descriptions supply most of this boost (+5.38 to +10.71); the scaffold changes the audio-augmented call by -0.79 to +2.54, partly through extra multi-label predictions, and evolved edits add at most +0.95, although no evolved increment survives Holm correction. The harness's emotion context also transfers to IntentQA, with small gains for three of four students, none of which survives correction. EmoHarness thus boosts small multimodal students chiefly by supplying and organizing audio-derived evidence.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11994) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11994)

## 58. MUTE: Multi-Level Alignment Uncoupling Against Talking-Head Exploitation for Voice Protection

- **作者**：Donghyun Kim, Jin Hong, Seungmin Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.SD, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. 33 pages, including appendix and checklist. Project and code: https://github.com/jindl465/MUTE

Talking-head generation models, which synthesize realistic facial animations from audio, are increasingly vulnerable to misuse in multimodal deepfake scenarios. Protecting such systems remains challenging, as talking-head models fundamentally rely on precise audio-visual alignment, while effective audio protection methods remain largely underexplored. In this work, we propose MUTE, an audio protection framework that uncouples the underlying audio-visual alignment through a multi-level strategy. Specifically, MUTE combines (1) Alignment-level Disruption, which directly perturbs cross-modal attention to disrupt structural alignment, and (2) Representation-level Degradation, which perturbs temporal audio embeddings to degrade their representations. To improve robustness, perturbations are constrained in the STFT domain to high-energy regions, making them resistant to post-processing, denoising, and adaptive attacks. An optional speaker-level objective further mitigates potential bypass via TTS-based resynthesis. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MUTE consistently degrades lip synchronization across both white-box and black-box talking-head models while preserving perceptual audio quality. The proposed method remains effective under real-world transformations and can be combined with image-based approaches to provide stronger multimodal protection. The source code and project page are available at https://github.com/jindl465/MUTE.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11989) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11989)

## 59. AnyBreak: Towards Jailbreaking Any T2I Model for Any Concept

- **作者**：Qi Lu, Zehui Guo, Yucheng Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted for poster presentation at ICME 2026

Text-to-image (T2I) models have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in image generation and editing, yet this progress has also introduced significant security risks. Prior work has shown that adversarial prompt can induce the generation of Not-Safe-For-Work (NSFW) content, including pornographic, violent, and graphic imagery. However, such attacks are typically mitigated by prompt filters and often rely on white-box assumptions. In this paper, we propose AnyBreak, a novel jailbreak framework that constructs adversarial images via multi-scale progressive semantic alignment. During the generation process, these adversarial images steer the generation trajectory in latent space towards unsafe data manifolds, enabling cross-modal transfer of malicious concepts from the text modality to the image modality. AnyBreak operates using only clean textual prompts and adversarial images at inference time, and can jailbreak any T2I models towards any malicious concepts in a fully black-box setting. We further introduce AnyBreak-V, an enhanced variant that incorporates a compensation term to bypass post-hoc safety checkers. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AnyBreak achieves high attack success rates while preserving visual fidelity and semantic consistency with the input prompts under black-box and clean-text constraints. Our findings reveal that existing T2I defense leave critical cross-modal attack surfaces insufficiently addressed. The code is available at https://github.com/luqi-glory/AnyBreak.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11980) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11980)

## 60. 3R-Adapter: Retrieval, Rewiring, and Refinement for Efficient Adaptation of 3D Reconstruction Model

- **作者**：Zixiang Ai, Zhenyu Cui, Xu Zou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted as NeurIPS 2026 Spotlight

Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models have demonstrated strong generalization under large-scale pre-training, yet they struggle in long-tail scenarios such as low-light reconstruction, joint human-scene reconstruction, and test-time adaptation. We observe relational attention structure collapse, manifested as the failure of cross-view correspondences, as a primary cause of these limitations. While adaptation offers a practical way to bridge this gap, existing parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) approaches mainly perform channel-wise feature adjustment and lack mechanisms to explicitly adapt relational structures, leaving correspondence failures unresolved. In this work, we formulate adaptation of 3D reconstruction models as a problem of correspondence recovery under domain shift and propose 3R-Adapter, a PEFT paradigm for long-tail 3D reconstruction scenarios. Our method consists of three components: Deformable Retrieval for task-adaptive relation candidate retrieval, Relational Rewiring for reconstructing cross-view relational structures, and Consistency Refinement for injecting the rewired relations into stable predictions. Extensive experiments on low-light reconstruction, joint human-scene reconstruction, and test-time adaptation demonstrate that our approach significantly improves robustness over existing PEFT methods while maintaining efficient lightweight adaptation. Our code is released at https://github.com/PKU-OV3-LAB/3R-Adapter.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11964) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11964)

## 61. Shaping Useful Noise: Energy Distributions Predict Visual Pretraining Quality

- **作者**：Ching Lam Choi, Antonio Torralba, Phillip Isola et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted as an oral presentation to NeurIPS 2026 (Sydney)

Synthetic data can scale visual pretraining, but current selection criteria only partially explain which datasets train strong representations. We propose using the energy profile of a dataset--the distribution of log-probability levels it occupies under a normalised real-image density model--as an audit and curation principle for synthetic data. To operationalise this view, we introduce Energy-Level Distribution Distance (ELD), which compares real and synthetic energy distributions. Real images span sparse high-likelihood scenes and dense low-likelihood textures, so useful synthetic data should cover this energy range rather than collapse to likely but low-variation samples. Across diverse synthetic datasets, ELD exposes mismatches missed by FID and predicts pretraining performance beyond FID, LPIPS variation, and feature-space recall, including across CNN and ViT self-supervised settings. We then use ELD to resample and mix synthetic sources, matching the real-image energy histogram while preserving diversity and improving downstream transfer.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11962) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11962)

## 62. UltraVoxelGS: Voxel-First Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting for 3D Ultrasound Reconstruction

- **作者**：Yue Wang, Haoxin Chen, Xiaoqing Guo
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.SP

We present UltraVoxelGS, a 3D ultrasound reconstruction method that achieves both fast reconstruction and physically faithful rendering for the first time, bringing live spatial feedback during 2D ultrasound scanning within reach. This is made possible by a feed-forward design that predicts a complete Gaussian scene representation from posed ultrasound images directly, entirely bypassing the per-scene optimization bottleneck that has confined prior methods to offline use. Realising such feed-forward prediction in this setting is non-trivial: unlike natural images, ultrasound slices share at most one-dimensional intersections and exhibit view-dependent appearance due to acoustic propagation and attenuation, violating the dense overlap and photometric consistency assumed by existing feed-forward Gaussian methods. Hence, to achieve fast reconstruction, we introduce a feed-forward voxel-to-Gaussian pass that decouples representation size from input count by lifting all posed slices into a fixed-size voxel support and predicting Gaussian parameters directly. For faithful rendering, we propose ultrasound-aware appearance rendering strategy factorize reflection from cumulative attenuation during training and exploit appearance continuity among spatially adjacent slices for efficient Render-time Appearance Adaptation. On both real-world and simulated datasets, UltraVoxelGS achieves best PSNR/SSIM compared with per-scene optimization baselines while reducing inference from 10-20 minutes to approximately 10 seconds (over 35 times faster) and scales to sequences exceeding 1,000 frames. Code is available at https://github.com/YuebyYue/UltraVoxelGS-camera-ready.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11951) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11951)

## 63. Watching Itself Watch: Self-Auditing Visual Reliance for Video Reasoning

- **作者**：Rohan Dugad, Mi Luo, Kristen Grauman
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://vision.cs.utexas.edu/projects/VIOS/

Video reasoning hinges on a small number of visually decisive tokens embedded in otherwise largely language-driven chains of thought. Yet prevailing post-training methods such as GRPO overlook this structure, assigning coarse trajectory-level rewards while requiring multiple rollouts and often costly external judges. We introduce Visually-Aware On-Policy Self-Distillation (VIOS), a post-training framework that identifies where a model under-utilizes visual evidence and concentrates learning at those positions. Our key insight is that a model can audit its own visual reliance: the divergence between its next-token distributions with and without the video reveals how strongly each prediction depends on the visual input. Measuring this reliance for both a student and an answer-privileged teacher exposes a per-token reliance gap, identifying positions where the teacher exploits visual evidence that the student overlooks. VIOS translates this signal into dense, targeted supervision through three complementary components: an exponential moving average (EMA) teacher that co-evolves with the student, a contrastive objective that encourages predictions to depend on the video, and a reliance-gap gate that focuses learning on visually under-utilized tokens. Crucially, VIOS requires only a single on-policy rollout from a single model, without external rewards, judges, or larger teachers. Across 12 benchmarks spanning general, temporal, spatial, knowledge-intensive, and long-video understanding, VIOS consistently improves three model backbones and outperforms contemporary video-reasoning systems, while training in over 9x fewer steps, 60x fewer FLOPs, and 111x fewer generated tokens. Project page: https://vision.cs.utexas.edu/projects/VIOS/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11949) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11949)

## 64. Scale-Consistent Monocular Fisheye Visual Odometry via Spherical Bundle Adjustment-Guided Unsupervised Depth Learning

- **作者**：Wanhui Luo, Hongbo Zhao, Yi Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：34 pages, 18 figures

The wide field of view of fisheye cameras and the simplicity of a monocular setup make monocular fisheye visual odometry (VO) attractive for robotic localization and mapping. Nevertheless, scale drift remains a major challenge over long trajectories. Unsupervised depth learning can help to mitigate this issue, but the pronounced distortion of fisheye images poses challenges for accurate depth estimation. This article presents S$^2$D-FVO, a monocular fisheye VO framework integrating spherical bundle adjustment (SBA)-guided unsupervised depth learning to improve scale consistency and depth estimation accuracy. Given that pixel reprojection residuals do not uniformly reflect angular deviations between viewing rays and that the first-order linearization of a scalar angular reprojection residual is insensitive to perturbations along one tangent direction, SBA employs tangent-space angular reprojection residuals derived from the spherical logarithmic map. These residuals retain sensitivity along both tangent directions, with their norms equal to the corresponding scalar angular residuals. A median depth-based scale regularization term is further integrated into SBA to constrain the reconstruction scale. Building on these formulations, SBA replaces the pose estimation network during unsupervised depth learning, providing more reliable relative poses for view synthesis. In turn, depth estimates from the well-trained depth estimation network provide scale references for SBA, improving scale consistency while allowing individual 3-D landmark positions to be refined. Extensive experiments conducted on multiple public datasets, as well as two newly collected large-scale datasets, demonstrate that the proposed framework outperforms representative existing approaches in both depth and trajectory estimation accuracy and is applicable to different camera models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11934) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11934)

## 65. Text Alone Predicts Which Classes a Segmentation Readout Keeps

- **作者**：David Yan
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

An open-vocabulary segmentation readout silently loses a class deployed beyond its training categories. It still emits masks with high confidence, but the class never wins a single pixel. The training loss falls anyway, and mIoU can even rise while classes die. We show that which classes collapse is written in the class-name list alone. Before any image is seen, we rank each class's collapse risk from its text similarity to the training vocabulary alone. The ranking holds at AUROC 0.69-0.96 across nine model families, twelve backbones and eight pretraining recipes. Two further families, measured only after the predictions were written down, land in the same band. What the list writes, it can rewrite. With identical images and a class's counterpart moved in or out of the training list, retraining flips that class's gain over the frozen baseline by 21 IoU points; swapping in unrelated names changes nothing. We find a vocabulary-distance mechanism behind the collapse. Collapsed classes lose every vote to near-name neighbors, but in the frozen features they survive. Turning down the readout's amplitude, or stopping training early, brings them back, up to +16 points, without retraining; trained with its amplitude held down, the readout never collapses.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11926) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11926)

## 66. LO-World: Unified BEV Latent Modeling for Joint 4D Occupancy and LiDAR Forecasting

- **作者**：Zhimin Liao, Ping Wei, Kai Mao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

LiDAR point clouds and 3D semantic occupancy represent two distinct modalities capturing fine-grained metric geometry and dense volumetric semantics, respectively, holding natural potential for mutual complementarity. However, existing world models typically forecast these modalities independently or cascade them heuristically, missing the opportunity for reciprocal cross-modal regularization. To address this, we propose \textbf{LO-World}, a unified 4D world model that forecasts both modalities within a shared BEV latent space. This single-stream formulation enables dense semantics and metric geometry to mutually reinforce each other, while eliminating multi-pipeline redundancy. Specifically, LO-World couples an efficient hybrid range-voxel autoencoder with a flow-matching generator that caches historical context to reduce iterative sampling latency. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LO-World outperforms prior state-of-the-art baselines, improving occupancy forecasting IoU by 10.9% and reducing LiDAR Chamfer Distance by 30.1%. Meanwhile, it sustains a real-time throughput exceeding $25$~FPS on a single RTX 4090. Our code is available at https://github.com/lzzzzzm/LO-World.

## 67. RBA: A Read-out Basis Adapter for Parameter-Efficient Interactive OCTA Vessel Segmentation with SEEM

- **作者**：Jaafar M. Al-Ghabban, Mohammad Reza Mohammadi
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Retinal disease monitoring with optical coherence tomography angiography (OCTA) relies on segmentation of vessels and the foveal avascular zone (FAZ). Promptable models such as SEEM (Segment Everything Everywhere All at Once) allow click-based refinement, but without adaptation SEEM segments thin vessels poorly and needs many clicks. Fully fine-tuning its 342 million parameters on a few hundred images is costly and risks overfitting, and parameter-efficient methods such as low-rank adaptation (LoRA) usually adapt only the encoder. Yet SEEM reads every candidate mask from one feature map at quarter resolution, too coarse for vessels one to three pixels wide. This study introduces the Read-out Basis Adapter (RBA), whose read-out branch generates 32 image-specific basis maps at half resolution. Each candidate accesses them through its own vector and a zero-initialised projection, so the model starts exactly as SEEM. The encoder branch, a standard rank-1 LoRA, supplies the information needed to tell arteries from veins. RBA adds 0.47 million trainable parameters. Under simulated error-driven clicks, with the ground truth used only to place them, RBA outperforms LoRA, Conv-LoRA andAdaptFormer on OCTA-500 3mmlarge vessels. Averaged over three seeds, it reaches an intersection over union (IoU) of 85.16% and a Dice score of 91.93%, 2.90 and 1.72 percentage points above Conv-LoRA. On the 6 mm subset, one run gives margins of 4.35 and 2.76 points. The gain extends to arteries, veins and ROSE-O but not to the FAZ. In SEEM, adaptation capacity pays more at the mask read-out than in the encoder.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11906) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11906)

## 68. Developing a Street View Imagery Processing Pipeline for Building-Level Vacant House Assessment

- **作者**：Dongjoon Park, Sunjae Lee, Jung Woo Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CY
- **备注**：51 pages, 9 figures, 3 tables, including appendices

Street view imagery (SVI) is widely used to audit urban environments remotely. Automated assessments usually assume that each image shows the target building clearly. In practice, an image may lack task-relevant evidence or show a neighboring house. If this goes unchecked, a model's score cannot show whether it judged the correct building. We present a reproducible SVI processing pipeline that makes image suitability and target identity explicit and checkable. We apply it to vacant house assessment in South Korea, using 10,311 images of 1,609 detached-house parcels. Task-suitability filtering adapts its criteria to image composition and administrative area. This keeps coverage even across areas. While keeping as many images as a single uniform filter, our filtering left 101 more parcels with a usable image. Target-house alignment identifies the parcel's house from camera metadata and parcel boundaries. It needs no building footprints or additional training. Alignment matched human labels for 85.6% of images, compared with 79.8% for the best simple rule. In vacancy classification, filtering and alignment raised AUROC from 0.684 to 0.719, but marking a different house in the same image still gave 0.709. Grad-CAM showed that the model often looked at areas outside the target house. Image suitability, target identification, and model evidence are therefore separate problems, and scores alone do not resolve them. Recording which images were used and which house was selected lets reviewers verify each assessment and can support human virtual audits.

## 69. Training Vision Transformers to Focus: Emergence of Spatial Attention Head Specialization

- **作者**：Yuji Nozawa, Yu-Chieh Lin, Youyang Ng
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://yuji-nozawa.github.io/ReFoCo/

Self-supervised learning in Vision Transformer models, such as DINO, enables robust visual perception without requiring labeled data by leveraging large-scale image datasets. However, these models lack a focusing mechanism during training, resulting in perceptual representations that are often ambiguous or overly similar. In this work, we introduce two visual focusing loss functions designed to establish correspondence between the model's attention and specific regions within images. Specifically, we leverage the multi-head attention mechanism within the model to selectively steer the model's focus, leading to the emergence of spatial attention head specialization, characterized by diverse and spatially focused perceptual capabilities without requiring annotations. Through both qualitative and quantitative evaluations, we demonstrate that our method substantially increases the spatial selectivity and diversity of attention heads, improving both interpretability and performance on dense prediction tasks such as zero-shot image segmentation.

## 70. How Many Candidates Does a One-Step World Model Need? A Random-Codebook Account of Nearest-of-m Training on Frozen DINO Tokens

- **作者**：Yong Hwi Song, Keon Myung Lee
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：30 pages (12-page main text, appendices A-H). Code: https://github.com/thddydgnl/nearest-of-m-world-models

One-step stochastic world models avoid the mode averaging of deterministic feature predictors without the tens of network evaluations per step that diffusion and flow matching require. The simplest such objective is nearest-of-m matching (conditional implicit maximum likelihood estimation, IMLE): draw m candidate futures and pull only the one closest to the observed outcome. Recent work conjectures that a small m suffices for feature-space world models and that a strictly proper energy score should replace the hard minimum. We test both claims with one-step world models on frozen DINOv2/v3 patch tokens, using benchmarks whose futures can be enumerated. Under mode-like uncertainty, nearest-of-m with m=16 covers the enumerated futures better than 50-step flow matching (at about 1/30 of its latency), one-step flow generators and an energy-score generator at matched parameter counts, and its rollouts stay on the token manifold where flow rollouts drift. One quantity governs the choice of m: the nearest-of-m objective is the expected distortion of a random codebook of m draws from the generator. This view yields three predictions, recorded before the corresponding runs: (i) memorization of the realized outcome in contexts that never repeat, whose strength is governed by the codebook's miss distortion I(m) rather than its miss probability, and which an energy-score generator does not escape by raising m; (ii) a flattening of mode probabilities with m, matching a known optimum within 0.01; and (iii) under-dispersion on continuous high-dimensional outcomes, which bounds the method on real robot video. Per-step matching on self-rollouts cuts long-horizon error by a third on a stochastic Push-T variant; an ablation separates its manifold and coverage benefits. Planning success does not improve despite accurate risk estimates, a negative result we report with its diagnosis.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11887) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11887)

## 71. Beyond Head Importance: Collective Computation in Video Transformers

- **作者**：Yi Yang, Siyuan Liu, Bingbing Nie
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Attention heads in video Transformers vary greatly in functional importance. Understanding their contributions requires examining not only which heads are important, but also how they participate in collective computation within the network. We study this collective computation from two perspectives: the downstream use of head outputs and the composition of interactions across layers. Ablation analyses of four video Transformers show that high-importance heads identified on a single task also support other video understanding tasks and help preserve the representational structure that distinguishes different videos. Representational responses to head ablation correlate weakly with head importance after the attention module, but more strongly after the MLP in the same block. Adapting this downstream processing through MLP-only fine-tuning further improves the use of lower-importance head outputs. Meanwhile, through composition across layers, sets of heads with shorter spatiotemporal attention distances support stronger perturbation responses at distant tokens than sets with longer distances. Their joint ablation causes even greater performance loss than ablating equally sized sets of high-importance heads, revealing collective contributions not fully captured by individual importance rankings. These findings highlight collective computation as a key perspective for understanding attention head function: functional contributions depend on both how head outputs are used downstream and how local interactions compose across layers.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11875) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11875)

## 72. Where Does Proposal Selection Fail? Diagnosing Frozen Vision-Language Queries over Detector Proposals

- **作者**：Bo Ma, Weiqi Yan, Jinsong Wu
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In modular grounding a frozen detector proposes regions and a vision-language model (VLM) selects one, at lower cost than letting the VLM decode a box, but it is unclear where such systems fail. We hold perception fixed---every trained selector chooses among the same frozen proposals, which contain the referent for at least $99.8\%$ of RefCOCO/+/g queries---and vary the selection interface one factor at a time for two VLMs of one family (Qwen2.5-VL-7B, Qwen3-VL-8B). On RefCOCOg, what the selector reads matters more than how it is built: the VLM's image-conditioned query encodes the referent's location and beats a text-only query by $0.15$--$0.18$ with every head tested (by less for InternVL3-8B, whose vision encoder differs; one head tested). The remaining errors are rankings among a few top candidates; a cross-attention selector supervised with every valid box reaches the VLM's coordinate decoding on RefCOCOg, and with Qwen2.5-VL on RefCOCO/+ when also trained on their data. Beyond that distribution it does not: on Ref-L4's Objects365 images, mostly of categories outside training, every trained head falls to $0.19$--$0.28$ while Qwen decoding keeps about $0.8$ (InternVL3's drops too), neither a wider detector prompt nor a class-agnostic proposer closes the gap, and on public reasoning benchmarks Ref-Adv-s and RefBench-PRO full-budget heads trail decoding by $0.19$--$0.37$ although proposals cover most referents. A trained verifier reduces to an existence probe on the VLM's query state and does not beat the VLM's yes/no answer on public no-target benchmarks.

## 73. Aggregation-Induced Calibration Failure in Sum-Based Label Expansion for Multi-Label Facial Expression Recognition: A Diagnostic Study

- **作者**：Weihang Yuan
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：13 pages, 3 figures, 14 tables. Code and data: https://github.com/yuanweihang810/CALE

Label-space expansion - predicting a larger joint space and summing back to the original labels - is a natural design for multi-label facial expression recognition, but its failure modes have not been characterised. We evaluate Condition-Aware Label Expansion (CALE) on a self-built dataset (30,240 training images, 3,460 multi-label test images), on seven backbones, and on the public RAF-DB and RAF-ML benchmarks. The central finding is that the aggregation rule, not the representation, decides whether the method works. With a fixed threshold of 0.5, the aggregate score spans 28x across five backbones at 10% of the training data (0.24 to 6.79) and collapses to a 1.9x range at full data, while threshold-free mAP stays within 1.28x over the same backbones, which localises the fault to the summation step. The mechanism is the uncalibrated aggregation rule, which adds the main-branch score to half the sum of the three non-main branches; we verify it by measuring main- and non-main-branch activations. Two plain seven-dimensional sigmoid baselines (BCE, ASL) reach mAP 0.5055 and 0.5175; ASL exceeds three of the five CALE configurations and falls within 0.03 of the best. On single-label FER the gain over a seven-dimensional baseline is nominally significant (paired t-test, p = 0.0436) but does not survive Benjamini-Hochberg correction (p_BH = 0.2055) or a Wilcoxon test (p = 0.1250). We conclude that sum-based label expansion requires threshold calibration at the deployment data scale, or a learned aggregation, before it can support multi-label decisions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11843) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11843)

## 74. MDA framework: multi-architecture domain adaptation for unsupervised person re-identification

- **作者**：Wei Zhang, Peijun Ye, Dihu Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Unsupervised person re-identification not only faces the challenge of domain gaps, but existing methods also rarely support multiple network architectures. To tackle both issues, this paper proposes a multi-architecture auto-adaptive domain adaptation method: Mamba augmented multi-architecture domain adaptation framework. Specifically, this paper constructs the multi-architecture domain adaptation capability at two levels. At the first level, we propose a composite operator(MDA operator) that encapsulates Mamba as a component for enhancing domain adaptation. At the second level, based on this operator, we propose a Mamba-enhanced multi-architecture Domain Adaptation framework(MDA framework). Comprehensive experiments on person re-identification benchmarks validate the consistency and flexibility of the proposed framework. The MDA framework supports versatile deployment strategies without being confined to a single integration point. Under a single deployment configuration, it surpasses several SOTA methods. Moreover, it demonstrates substantial performance improvements across both CNN and Transformer architectures. When embedded into a CNN backbone, the framework achieves a 10.2% mAP improvement on the adaptation task from CUHK03 to Market-1501. Integrated with a Transformer backbone, it yields a 19.8% mAP improvement on the same task.

## 75. Answer or Defer? Testing VLMs' Stated Confidence on Brain MRI

- **作者**：Amir Sabbaghziarani, Parsa Ghazvinian, Sepehr Salem et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted to the WACV 2027 FABRIC Workshop

Vision-language models (VLMs) could support medical-image interpretation, but their use requires deciding which interpretations to accept and which images to refer to a clinician. We test whether thresholding stated confidence can retain useful coverage while limiting errors among accepted answers, and how this depends on the population. Eight open-weight VLMs, including two reasoning models, assess 3,888 brain MRI slices from post-treatment glioma patients (BraTS 2024) and healthy volunteers (IXI) for tumor or gross abnormality. BraTS labels are mask-derived; IXI slices are treated as negatives. On BraTS, no threshold selects a nonempty set with at most 5% empirical error for any model, even when chosen retrospectively. This also holds for four models evaluated on a large-lesion, distant-negative subset. At a 10% error budget, Gemma-4-12B retains 24.4% of slices and every other model at most 2.5%. To investigate this limited selection ability, we separate confidence differences between predicted answers from differences within each answer. For four models with available outcome-level summaries, pooled mean confidence differs more between answers than between correct and incorrect responses within each answer. A covariance decomposition explains how this pattern can produce opposite confidence-correctness associations across populations. At a 20% error budget, accepting every "tumor" answer covers more slices than any confidence threshold for two models. These findings caution against using unvalidated stated confidence as a standalone deferral signal and support population-specific validation before deployment. Code is available at https://github.com/amir-sbg/VLM-Answer-or-Defer.

## 76. End-to-End Joint Training of Super-Resolution and Compression Artifact Removal for Industrial Machine Vision

- **作者**：Dang BuiDinh, Sang NguyenQuang, Anh NguyenPhuong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In industrial machine vision, images captured by edge devices are often downsampled and compressed to reduce bandwidth, introducing resolution loss and compression artifacts that severely degrade the performance of downstream tasks, such as defect detection. Existing quality enhancement methods typically apply super-resolution (SR) or compression artifact removal (CAR) independently or in a sequential manner and are largely optimized for human visual fidelity rather than machine perception. This paper proposes an end-to-end quality enhancement framework that integrates SR and CAR with a unified training strategy that optimizes both human-perception and machine-perception objectives. We investigate fixed-weight and uncertainty-based dynamic-weight loss functions to balance reconstruction quality and machine vision task performance. Extensive experiments on PCB Defect, DeepPCB, and NEU-DET datasets show that the proposed jointly trained model generally outperforms standalone and separately trained SR-CAR pipelines. In particular, under severe JPEG compression, our method improves defect detection performance from 0.461 to 0.887 mAP@50 on PCB Defect over sequential SR-CAR baselines, with only a slight reduction in PSNR. These results demonstrate that joint optimization is critical for compression-aware industrial machine vision systems.

## 77. When to Trust Adaptation: Contextual Fusion for Transferable Vision-Language Prompting

- **作者**：Zhenyuan Chen, Wenshuo Tao, Zhuo Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

When a vision-language model encounters a new class or domain, how much should it trust its learned prompts? Because source adaptation can correct pretrained errors on some target images while disturbing valid predictions on others, we introduce Contextual Adaptive Fusion (CAF), which retains four prediction branches by pairing frozen and prompted visual and textual representations. To arbitrate when branches disagree on an image, CAF combines each branch's predictive confidence with how frequently the branches favor its proposed class across the unlabeled target collection, placing greater trust in predictions that are locally confident yet less dominated by collection-wide frequency bias. The fusion coefficients are learned once on source data and remain fixed at inference. Trained on ImageNet, CAF achieves 69.96% average accuracy across ten cross-dataset benchmarks (1.92 percentage points above ManiPT) and remains highly competitive across four ImageNet variants, setting a new best of 52.21% on ImageNet-S. In base-to-novel transfer across eleven datasets, CAF further reaches 79.77% novel-class accuracy and an 82.81% harmonic mean. Code is available at https://github.com/zhenyuanchenai/CAF.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11824) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11824)

## 78. Direct Trajectory Optimization for Inference-Time Alignment of Text-to-Image Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Huixuan Zhang, Xiaojun Wan
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Alignment of text-to-image diffusion models with reward functions is a key challenge in controllable generation. Existing approaches typically rely on optimizing model inputs or fine-tuning denoising networks, which requires computationally expensive backpropagation through large denoising models. In this work, we propose Direct Trajectory Optimization (DTO), a training-free framework that performs alignment by directly optimizing the sampling trajectory of diffusion models. Specifically, DTO treats intermediate denoising scores and sampled noise as the optimization variables, eliminating the need for gradients through the denoising network. To mitigate the out-of-distribution problem, we introduce Reward-Optimized Score Guidance, which interpolates between the optimized scores and the pretrained model predictions to stabilize sampling, while providing a provable theoretical guarantee of reward improvement under constrained distributions. Empirically, DTO improves alignment performance across three diffusion models on two benchmarks, outperforming existing inference-time alignment methods while maintaining high efficiency. These results suggest that trajectory-level optimization is a promising direction for efficient and effective diffusion model alignment.

## 79. Budget-aware Correspondence-driven View Selection for Real-time 3D Perception of Mobile Robots

- **作者**：Vaishnav Ramesh, Jacob Carter, Shivali J. Patel et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages

3D visual perception and scene reconstruction are critical for autonomous robots and intelligent transportation, yet real-world deployments often generate long, redundant image sequences that hinder downstream processing. This report investigates BCVS (budget-aware correspondence-driven view sampling), a pose-free view selection algorithm that offers 22+ FPS on a CPU, enabling online filtering for efficient 3D perception by mobile robots. Unlike existing approaches that rely on full structure-from-motion (SfM) pipelines or camera pose estimation, BCVS employs a purely correspondence-based scoring strategy that prioritizes view novelty while maintaining sufficient overlap for geometric stability. Given a user-specified budget, BCVS iteratively selects the most distinct frames and suppresses near-duplicates, producing compact subsets that preserve connectivity and minimize reconstruction loss. Extensive experiments on benchmark show that BCVS outperforms CPU-friendly baselines at tight budgets and remains competitive as budgets scale. Field trials with large aerial and marine robotics data further validate its practicality and scalability: BCVS reduces redundancy up to 16% while sustaining robust SfM registration and high-quality 3D reconstructions.

## 80. Benchmarking CNNs, Vision Transformers, and Foundation Models for Explainable Brain Tumor MRI Classification: Calibration, Decision Analysis, and External Validation

- **作者**：Akwasi Asare
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Under review at Scientific Report Journal, Springer

Deep learning models for brain tumor MRI classification are usually compared by accuracy alone, on public datasets whose independence is rarely checked. We benchmarked six architectures: two convolutional networks (EfficientNetV2-M and ConvNeXt-V2-Tiny), two vision transformers (Swin-V2-S and ViT-L/16), and two foundation models (DINOv2-L and MedSigLIP-448), for four-class classification of 7,216 public MRI images. We assessed accuracy, calibration, explainability, ensembles, and decision curves, with every fitted quantity estimated on validation data only. Pixel-verified duplicate screening showed that 36% of the test images had a near-duplicate in the training data. Test accuracy ranged from 94.5% to 95.6%, with no pairwise difference after Holm correction, and fell to 90.3% to 91.7% on the 773 test images free of duplicates and shared patients. Ensembles did not outperform the best single model. Attribution maps passed a model-randomization test but localized expert-annotated tumors poorly. On 1,675 external BDNeuro-MRI images that did not duplicate the training data, accuracy ranged from 77.6% (MedSigLIP-448) to 95.0% (ViT-L/16), and 12 of 15 pairwise differences were significant. Internal accuracy on this public collection therefore did not distinguish the architectures, whereas the duplicate-filtered external test did.

## 81. FMA-Net: Flexible Modality Adaptation for Robust Multimodal Face Presentation Attack Detection

- **作者**：Yuhao Gao, Yichen Shi, Zitong Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Multimodal face presentation attack detection (fPAD) improves robustness using complementary RGB, depth, and infrared (IR) cues, but deployment remains challenging when modalities are unavailable or incomplete. Existing flexible-modality methods mainly use zero-filling or modality omission, potentially discarding cross-modal information and introducing modality-dependent bias. We propose the Flexible Modality Adaptation Network (FMA-Net), a unified framework that compensates for missing modalities through instruction-guided latent-space translation. The Face Modality Instruct Translator (FMIT) estimates target modality observations from RGB inputs, while context-aware category enhancement uses learnable text prompts as modality-invariant semantic anchors. A Facial Feature Adapter (F2A) integrates global facial structure and local spoof-relevant cues to enhance discriminative representations. Synthetic-to-Real Face Domain Alignment (SR-FDA) reduces feature discrepancies between translated and real modalities, with benefits depending on the missing-modality pattern and data domain. Experiments on WMCA and PADISI-Face validate the framework. On WMCA with a 70% modality missing ratio, FMA-Net achieves a mean ACER of 3.22% across four intra-domain missing-modality protocols, compared with 4.24% for the strongest baseline by the same criterion. When both depth and IR are entirely unavailable, it achieves 5.23% ACER, compared with 5.99% for the best competing result. Cross-domain experiments also reveal limitations of synthetic-to-real alignment under dataset shifts. These results support FMA-Net as a practical approach to fPAD with flexible and incomplete modalities.

## 82. Towards Rotation-Only Imaging Geometry: Multi-View Pose Estimation

- **作者**：Xinrui Li, Qi Cai, Yuanxin Wu
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Global structure-from-motion (SfM) commonly refines relative poses on individual image pairs and then estimates global rotations by rotation averaging. Pairwise refinement does not explicitly combine feature-level constraints with multi-view consistency during rotation initialization. We introduce Global Rotations from Aggregated Matches (GRAM), which estimates global rotations directly from initial relative rotations and inlier matches by minimizing edge-wise rotation-only algebraic residuals. GRAM replaces two-view relative-pose optimization and rotation averaging with a single stage. We further extend rotation- only imaging geometry to multiple views and develop Multi-view Optimization with Rotation-only Reprojection Error (MORE) for nonlinear global rotation refinement. Analytical Rotation-guided Translation (ART) derives locally scaled triplet translations from Linear Global Translation (LiGT) constraints and recovers global translations using weighted least squares. Synthetic and real-world experiments demonstrate the accuracy, robustness, and efficiency of the proposed methods.

## 83. LangDepth: Vision-Language Scene Priors for Semantic Binning and Cross-Domain Monocular Depth Estimation

- **作者**：Reyhaneh Ahani Manghotay, Jie Liang
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Monocular depth estimation has advanced through large-scale pretraining and strong visual backbones, yet metric depth remains sensitive to scene-level scale. Indoor rooms and outdoor driving scenes occupy very different depth ranges, but most depth decoders infer this global structure implicitly from visual features. We ask whether scene-level semantic structure can instead act as an upstream prior that shapes the metric output space before dense prediction. We present LangDepth (SemanticBins), a monocular depth architecture that couples strong visual features with a scene-conditioned output space. A frozen CLIP encoder and a learnable bank of scene- and geometry-oriented concept keys convert image features into a compact scene descriptor, without requiring per-image text or an external captioner. A Scene Prior Module (SPM) maps this descriptor to a scene-conditioned metric depth interval, providing a global range anchor before dense decoding. In parallel, a DINOv2-L backbone with a lightweight FPN extracts dense visual features, and a Per-Pixel Assignment Network (PPAN) assigns pixels to the scene-prescribed bin structure. A confidence gate fuses the dense visual estimate with the SPM prior. We train LangDepth jointly on NYU Depth V2 and KITTI under a modest supervised depth budget, without web-scale depth supervision or pseudo-labelled depth data. A single model achieves delta_1 = 0.960 on NYU, 0.960 on KITTI, 0.887 on DIODE indoor, 0.946 on iBims-1, and 0.861 on SUN RGB-D. Beyond aggregate accuracy, the SPM predicts compact indoor ranges and much wider outdoor KITTI ranges, the gate becomes SPM-dominant on outdoor scenes, and perturbing the routed scene context causes large drops on KITTI and iBims-1. These results support scene-conditioned output spaces as a practical and interpretable mechanism for cross-domain monocular depth estimation.

## 84. Calibrated Alarm Thresholds for Compact Fire and Smoke Detectors

- **作者**：Vikas Sandireddy
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Compact object detectors such as YOLOv8n are a common choice for camera-based fire and smoke detection on low-power devices. These detectors are usually evaluated with box-level metrics such as mean average precision, and their outputs are turned into decisions with a fixed confidence threshold such as 0.25 or 0.5. A fire monitoring system, however, makes one decision per image: raise an alarm or not. In this paper we evaluate two publicly available compact detectors on the D-Fire test set at the image level. We compare three simple rules for turning box detections into one alarm score per image, and we use a split conformal procedure to choose the alarm threshold from held-out images so that the expected fraction of missed fire and smoke images stays below a chosen target. We find that the same fixed threshold leads to very different miss rates for the two detectors, 17.3 and 8.4 percent at a threshold of 0.5, while the calibrated threshold meets miss-rate targets of 10, 5 and 1 percent for both detectors and all three rules. At a 5 percent target, the false alarm rate on images without fire or smoke stays between 1.2 and 3.0 percent. We also find that smoke-only images stay below the target even when the overall target is met, and we describe the main failure cases.

## 85. SpinSCI: Mask-Conditioned Spatial-Temporal Spin Transformer for Video Snapshot Compressive Imaging

- **作者**：Seungki Hong, Zhishuai Zhang, Taekwang Jang
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video snapshot compressive imaging (SCI) recovers multiple frames from one spatiotemporally coded measurement. This paper proposes SpinSCI, a mask-conditioned Spatial-Temporal Spin Transformer whose Mask-Conditioned Ising Refinement Block (MC-IRB) combines attention logits and sensing information to form signed spin couplings. Finite mean-field updates gate residual feature refinement. Physics-guided initialization and final data consistency incorporate the measurement model. Compared with HiSViT$_9$, SpinSCI achieves average PSNR gains of 0.51 dB, 0.23 dB, and 0.04 dB on small-scale gray, mid-scale color, and large-scale color benchmarks, respectively, with 16.9% fewer parameters, 21.0-21.6% fewer FLOPs, 17.1-49.9% lower latency, and 62.3-63.3% lower peak memory. Relative to DU-9stg, average PSNR differs by at most 0.13 dB across these benchmarks, with 28.2-28.7% fewer FLOPs, 64.6-71.7% lower inference latency, and 30.1-35.0% lower peak memory. Qualitative comparisons on the real benchmark further show recovery of fine details and textures. Code and reference checkpoints are available at https://github.com/skethz/SpinSCI.

## 86. GeoPilot: Diagnosis-Guided Interventions and Geometric Evaluation for Aerial Reconstruction

- **作者**：Pengyu Yin, Jie Pan, Junxiang Tan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-09 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：33 pages, including supplementary material

When a mesh reconstructed from aerial remote-sensing imagery disagrees with reference geometry, deciding what to change requires a diagnosis of the reconstruction process. We present GeoPilot, a workflow that turns diagnostics and prior observations into executable program or camera-configuration interventions. A large language model proposes the change, numerical tools reconstruct the mesh, and an external evaluator measures its geometry under a stated coordinate and reference protocol. The main case tests a shared-intrinsics hypothesis proposed before the comparison. On 224 Metashape-raster development images, shared intrinsics improved all six native-coordinate measures and reduced mean surface-to-reference distance by 7.04%. Reference-assisted alignment fitted and evaluated on separate within-scene checkerboard samples reversed the mean-distance ranking, limiting that gain to native-frame delivery. A separate matched comparison on publisher-undistorted images favored fixed published cameras on all six measures, reducing mean distance by 12.11%; the cameras retain upstream image and laser-scanning adjustment information. With one reconstruction per configuration, these comparisons provide conditional evidence for camera handling. Three end-to-end runs each delivered a mesh and six-metric evaluation from all 327 images of a second development scene.
