## 220. Split and Bridge: Multimodal Generation via Diffusion Bridging

- **作者**：Ahmad Arrabi, Xiaohan Zhang, Xingyu Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

Visual data are often expressed through multiple complementary modalities (e.g., images and segmentation masks), each capturing different aspects of the same underlying structure. Most existing multimodal methods adopt a top-down paradigm, learning a shared latent variable through joint training from scratch, which limits scalability. We propose Split and Bridge (SnB), a bottom-up framework for multimodal generation that couples pretrained unimodal diffusion models at sampling time. Rather than learning a unified latent space, SnB generates coherent multimodal samples by following a shared diffusion trajectory up to a split point and then branching into modality-specific trajectories guided by a bridge module. This design enables plug-and-play reuse of powerful pretrained models without joint training, making it scalable and easily adaptable. Across standard benchmarks (PolyMNIST, CelebAMask-HQ) and more complex settings (PIE-Bench++, ROCO), SnB achieves a strong balance between sample quality and coherence. We show that SnB naturally extends to downstream zero-shot tasks such as interleaved co-generation, image editing, and style coupling/decoupling. Our results demonstrate that bottom-up generation provides a practical framework that complements existing top-down multimodal approaches. Code is available at https://github.com/AhmadArrabi/SnB

## 221. Mental Imagery That Matters: Curing Latent Collapse in Multimodal Reasoning

- **作者**：Mi Luo, Rohan Dugad, Zihui Xue et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Accepted by NeurIPS 2026, Project page: https://vision.cs.utexas.edu/projects/AnchorSim/

Mental simulation, the ability to reason by constructing and updating internal visual states, is central to flexible human cognition, but remains elusive in current multimodal large language models. Latent multimodal reasoning offers a promising route toward this capability by interleaving text with continuous visual latent blocks, allowing models to maintain internal visual workspaces without decoding them into pixels. In this paper, we ask whether these latent visual states actually matter. Through a direct perturbation analysis across representative latent-reasoning MLLMs, we find that they often do not: dropping, replacing, or shuffling generated visual latents has only minor effects on the model's own final-answer probability, while equivalent perturbations to the linguistic reasoning trace are substantially more damaging. We call this failure mode Mental Simulation Collapse (MSC): the model appears to perform interleaved visual reasoning, but routes most answer-relevant computation through text. We further diagnose that MSC follows naturally from existing training objectives: visual alignment can make generated latents resemble helper-image embeddings, but it does not require the decoder to rely on them; subsequent text-only supervision further leaves the semantic role of the latent pathway unidentifiable. To address this, we introduce AnchorSim (Anchored Mental Simulation), a contrastive distillation framework that trains models to make their generated visual latents consequential. Across multiple visual reasoning benchmarks, AnchorSim improves performance, while diagnostic re-evaluation shows that visual perturbations now meaningfully affect predictions. Our results expose a fundamental failure mode of latent multimodal reasoning and demonstrate that mental simulation must be anchored to model behavior in order to matter.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10423) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10423)

## 222. S2HAND: Accuracy-Preservation Trade-Offs for Hand Specialization in Whole-Body Pose Models

- **作者**：Singyu Muk, Xuanren Song, Kalin Stefanov
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：39 pages, including appendices. Code and models: https://github.com/ronggong000/S2HAND

Unified whole-body pose estimators jointly predict body, face, and hand landmarks, but often degrade on small, blurred, or occluded hands. Attaching a standalone hand specialist introduces extra detection overhead, coordinate alignment, and wrist-stitching artifacts. We introduce S2HAND, a hand-specialized Sapiens2-5B framework that preserves the native 308-keypoint output interface. Specifically, S2HAND-MT adapts a lightweight 0.87M-parameter pose branch to route 40 finger heatmaps while strictly preserving the shared Transformer, auxiliary task heads, and the other 268 body, foot, and face heatmaps. S2HAND-Pose further fine-tunes three Fisher-selected Transformer blocks (267.13M parameters), trading exact preservation for stronger hand recovery: it lifts macro AUC from 57.83% to 65.73% across HInt TEST, COCO WholeBody VAL, and FreiHAND EVAL, and surpasses HInt-adapted Hand5/HRNet and stitched Hand5 systems on all five Native Pipeline benchmarks. On COCO, S2HAND-Pose improves hand AP by 12.89 points and body-plus-foot AP by 0.19 points. Across a 512-image multi-task audit, it maintains 99.60% foreground segmentation agreement with the public checkpoint, with quantified normal and pointmap drift. Its 2D coordinates also lower 3D mesh error in frozen Pose2Mesh transfer, while our optional TailGuard re-cropping module cuts S2HAND-MT's severe failure rate on NewDays from 13.45% to 10.43%. S2HAND thus resolves the whole-body hand bottleneck with either exact non-hand preservation (MT) or empirically audited trade-offs (Pose).

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10416) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10416)

## 223. Frequency-Structure Complementary Modeling for Hyperspectral Image Fusion

- **作者**：Song Qiya, Tian Siyu, Li Shutao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Existing methods mainly rely on convolutional aggregation or attention-based spatial-spectral interaction. However, conventional feature-domain attention does not explicitly characterize variation patterns along spatial-token and learned latent-channel coordinates, while spatially shared local operators lack the flexibility to adapt neighborhood aggregation to heterogeneous structures. To address these limitations, we propose a Frequency-Structure Complementary Modeling Network (FSCM-Net), which couples frequency-aware dependency learning with structure-adaptive local refinement. Specifically, a Spatial-Spectral Frequency Calibration Attention (SSFCA) module constructs spatial-token affinities from Fourier-interacted query-key representations and builds latent-channel affinities after input-conditioned frequency filtering along learned channel coordinates. The latent-channel response is then calibrated by a learned shared gain and combined with the spatial-token response. Complementarily, a Structure-Coherent Dynamic Feature Fusion (SC-DFF) module combines regularized structure-tensor cues from MSI features with context-modulated HSI detail representations to generate channel- and position-dependent aggregation kernels. This design makes local HSI refinement responsive to smooth regions, edges, and textures while maintaining spectral fidelity. The two modules are progressively integrated within a multiscale encoder-decoder to coordinate representation-level interaction and structure-adaptive reconstruction. Experiments on two simulated benchmarks and one real remote-sensing dataset demonstrate favorable performance against representative fusion methods in spatial detail reconstruction and spectral preservation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10413) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10413)

## 224. Tiresias: Towards Language-Conditioned World Models

- **作者**：Fengyi Wu, Yifei Dong, Yetong Sha et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：33 pages, including appendix and references. Code: https://github.com/UWMILab/Tiresias

World models enable planning through internal simulation, while natural language provides intuitive task specification. Yet, language-conditioned world modeling remains fragmented across domains with disparate action spaces, datasets, and evaluation protocols. We introduce LCWM, a shared task that maps an initial agent-view observation and a language instruction to an action sequence that fulfills the instruction. Its accompanying benchmark, LCWM-Bench, integrates 15 datasets across navigation, robotics, 3D gaming, and driving under a shared action interface and evaluation protocol, pairing 142,987 episodes with 428,961 instructions in three styles. We further propose Tiresias, an agent that plans via selective imagination: a language-conditioned diffusion policy generates candidate trajectories, a trajectory ranker shortlists them, and a video-pretrained world model imagines their visual consequences. A visual ranker evaluates these rollouts, complementing trajectory scores to select the final plan. Tiresias achieves competitive planning and imagination performance on LCWM-Bench. Ablations demonstrate the complementary benefits of trajectory ranking and visual verification, while experiments with alternative proposal methods show that the trained rankers remain effective beyond our diffusion policy. Together, LCWM-Bench and Tiresias provide a unified evaluation setting and an effective starting point for studying language-guided planning through world models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10392) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10392)

## 225. From Reflection to Co-Evolution: Self-Evolving Agents with Locally Verified Memory

- **作者**：Xinxiang Yin, Xinglang Zhang, Haodong Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：31 pages, 9 figures, 6 tables

LLM agents have made impressive progress in coding and mathematical reasoning, motivating growing interest in learning from long-horizon interaction. Memory supports this process, but plausible lessons can be redundant or reinforce inappropriate actions. Can a memory's source decision provide a practical test for whether to retain it? We localize consequential trajectory steps, extract candidate memories, and replay each source decision with and without its candidate under the same context. This reveals two properties: most candidates barely shift the probed distribution, and both successful and failed source actions can be reinforced or suppressed. These observations motivate Anchor, which tests shift magnitude and source-role alignment before retention. Admitted memories guide later trajectories, enabling experience-driven improvement under a frozen policy. On held-out ALFWorld and WebShop tasks, Anchor improves success rate and mean score by up to 19.9% and 17.2%, respectively, relative to the strongest baseline for each backbone. With Qwen3.5-27B, it retains 41% fewer ALFWorld cards than unfiltered retention and outperforms all four WebShop cost-comparison baselines while using fewer tokens.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10382) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10382)

## 226. RefineSplat: Reconstruction-Guided Object-Centric Gaussian Splatting for Open-Vocabulary Querying

- **作者**：Yiren Lu, Disheng Liu, Sreya Srinidhi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Open-vocabulary 3D scene understanding aims to segment and retrieve arbitrary objects in 3D scenes using natural language queries. Most existing methods typically follow a splat-then-segment paradigm, where 3D Gaussian Splatting is first reconstructed and then augmented with labels or language features for subsequent segmentation. However, this pipeline often suffers from view inconsistency, ambiguous object boundaries, and weak object-level correspondence, especially in complex static and dynamic scenes. Building on our prior Segment-then-Splat framework, we present RefineSplat, a reconstruction-guided extension that retains its object-centric paradigm: it first tracks objects in 2D and then reconstructs each object with a dedicated set of 3D Gaussians, preserving explicit object-Gaussian correspondence for unified open-vocabulary segmentation and retrieval. To improve robustness, we further close the loop between 2D tracking and 3D reconstruction. Since off-the-shelf video trackers can produce unreliable masks that propagate errors into object reconstruction, we introduce a reconstruction-guided feedback mechanism that refines object tracking using rendered masks and multi-view consistency. Moreover, instead of assigning each object a single language embedding, we propose a viewpoint-aware embedding assignment strategy, where each object maintains multiple view-clustered embeddings to capture view-dependent semantic cues. We also introduce Blender-OVS, a synthetic benchmark of static and dynamic sequences with exact object meshes and point clouds, enabling direct evaluation of extracted geometry. Experiments on static and dynamic benchmarks show improved rendered-view segmentation and lower object-level Chamfer distances, with competitive scene reconstruction quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10379) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10379)

## 227. Vision Language Models for Primary Structure Threat Identification in Wildland Urban Interface Fires

- **作者**：Zhangding Liu, Neda Mohammadi, John E. Taylor
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：6 pages, 3 figures, 1 table. Accepted at the 2026 IEEE International Smart Cities Conference (ISC2), Porto, Portugal

Wildland urban interface (WUI) fires increasingly threaten communities and infrastructure, motivating the need for rapid visual assessment. Recent research has focused primarily on detecting fire and assessing post-event damage. However, structure-level fire threat identification during active WUI fire events remains underexplored. This study evaluates Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct for this active-fire decision using two separately scored tracks: 1,824 synthetic images from 491 designed WUI scenarios and 45 frames from a controlled-burn experiment conducted in collaboration with Columbus Fire & EMS Department. Under zero-shot prompting, the model returned valid target fields for 1,808 synthetic images and 39 controlled-burn frames. Accuracy among valid outputs was 94.0% for the synthetic track and 69.2% for the controlled-burn track, compared with a 16.0% random-selection baseline for the latter. On the synthetic track, the model generally identified the intended target across varied designed WUI scenarios when the relevant fire-spread cues were visually distinct. Controlled-burn performance was well above random selection, but errors showed difficulty interpreting spatial relations in complex scenes. The dual-track protocol provides scalable stress testing in designed scenarios and a controlled-burn reference for assessing model behavior under physical fire conditions. Future work can use the synthetic data for VLM post-training and then evaluate the adapted models on larger and more diverse real WUI fire datasets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10377) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10377)

## 228. PhysDNet: Physics-Driven Gradient Amplification for Real-Time Image Dehazing

- **作者**：Rayen Dhahri, Lei Sun, Victor Javier Kartsch Morinigo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026. Code, deployment scripts and pre-trained checkpoints: https://github.com/Heatdh/PhysDnet

Modern dehazing networks are often computationally expensive and overfit to synthetic haze, limiting real-time deployment. This underscores an urgent demand for efficient, high-fidelity restoration methods that can operate on resource-constrained edge devices in practical, dynamic environments. We introduce PhysDNet, a lightweight multimodal architecture that fuses NIR imagery with sparse LiDAR depth and is trained with a physics-constrained dual-head objective. Differentiating through a fixed Koschmieder inversion induces density-adaptive gradient amplification, acting as an implicit resource allocation mechanism that concentrates learning on heavily degraded regions without increasing inference cost. PhysDNet delivers superior restoration with minimal capacity and enhanced training efficiency, facilitating real-time deployment on edge devices without increasing inference overhead. On the Seeing Through Fog benchmark, PhysDNet outperforms competitors by 1.67 dB at 5.2 times lower compute while recovering 54.9% of fog-induced detection loss. Ablations and cross-domain evaluations show that physics-guided training improves generalization, including under non-homogeneous haze that violates the Koschmieder assumption. PhysDNet further demonstrates compatibility with fixed-function NPUs, running at 29/45/99 FPS at <4 W across L/M/S variants. Code, deployment scripts and pre-trained checkpoints are available at https://github.com/Heatdh/PhysDnet.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10376) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10376)

## 229. ClearForm: Shared-Surface Gaussian Splatting for Transparent and Opaque Scenes

- **作者**：Hajeong Lee, Jong-Il Park
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 9 figures

Transparent-interface reconstruction must recover glass surfaces even when most observed color comes from the scene behind them. We present ClearForm, a shared-planar-Gaussian method for jointly reconstructing transparent interfaces and the appearance seen through them. We identify and analyze the Geometry-Appearance Compositing Dilemma (GACD): when the same transparency-dependent alpha controls both color and geometry, revealing the background can weaken a transparent surface's contribution to geometric reconstruction. We derive its effect on plane-based depth estimation, identify a mechanism that can weaken geometric learning, and quantify the loss of interface support through controlled comparisons. To address this conflict, we develop geometry-preserving dual-opacity surface reconstruction. Shared planar Gaussians retain interface contributions in geometry compositing while modeling transmission and sparse reflection in RGB, supported by transparency-aware geometric constraints. To manage the different primitive-visibility requirements of these compositors, we introduce Dual-Evidence Lifecycle (DEL), which retains Gaussians supported by multi-view geometry or RGB reconstruction evidence. On 3D-FRONT-T, ClearForm achieves 27.05 mm Chamfer distance (CD) and 0.928 F1, compared with 59.18 mm CD for our PGSR reproduction and the reported 34 mm CD and 0.836 F1 for GLINT. On TransLab, it achieves 1.65 mm CD, 0.963 F1, and 40.14 dB PSNR. In matched ablations, dual-opacity compositing reduces CD from 59.25 to 27.09 mm, while DEL improves PSNR by 2.06 dB. Our project page is available at https://hajeongx.github.io/clearform/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10370) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10370)

## 230. DAPointRWKV: Domain Adaptive Vision RWKV for Point Cloud Completion

- **作者**：Yinghui Li, Qianyu Zhou, Hao Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：This paper has been accepted by 34th ACM International Conference on Multimedia

Domain adaptive point cloud completion (DA PCC) aims to bridge the geometric discrepancy between labeled source domains and unlabeled target domains for accurate 3D shape completion. Existing methods based on CNNs, Transformers, and Mamba-like sequence models still face challenges from limited receptive fields, high computational complexity, insufficient geometric bias, and serialization sensitivity. Recently, Receptance Weighted Key Value (RWKV) provides a linear-complexity alternative for long-range sequence modeling. However, adapting RWKV to DA PCC remains challenging due to the lack of explicit geometric priors, the mismatch between recurrent modeling and coarse-to-fine generation, and unstable target-domain adaptation. To address these challenges, we propose DAPointRWKV, a novel RWKV-based framework for DA PCC. DAPointRWKV consists of three components: Geometry-Aware RWKV (GA-RWKV) Backbone, RWKV-Oriented Progressive Decoder (RWKV-PD), and Dual Structural Regularization (DSR). Specifically, GA-RWKV introduces geometry-aware recurrent representation learning, RWKV-PD bridges RWKV token representations with coarse-to-fine reconstruction through local token-guided refinement, and DSR stabilizes target-domain transfer through visible-structure anchoring and complete-shape hypothesis stabilization. Extensive experiments on synthetic and real-world benchmarks demonstrate that DAPointRWKV consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10353) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10353)

## 231. SituRecBench: Evaluating Frontier Models as Situated Recommendation Assistants in 3D Environments

- **作者**：Jingtong Yang, Dongding Lin, Jian Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.CV, cs.IR, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted by NeurIPS 2026 Evaluations and Datasets Track

Recommender systems are transitioning from static retrieval engines into interactive assistants grounded in physical environments. However, current studies largely neglect the combined complexities of spatial navigation, visual grounding, and multi-turn social interaction. To bridge this gap, we introduce SituRecBench, an interactive benchmark for evaluating frontier vision-language models (VLMs) as situated recommendation assistants within high-fidelity 3D environments. We reformulate recommendation as a situated assistance process in which an assistant interacts with a user while sharing the user's egocentric view. Our benchmark simulates real-world shopping environments (e.g., supermarkets and home furnishings stores), requiring assistants to elicit preferences, ground recommendations in the environment, and guide the user to relevant locations through dialogue. The benchmark evaluates dialogue quality, recommendation utility, and navigation effectiveness through a unified interactive protocol. Extensive experiments reveal persistent challenges in spatial grounding and preference elicitation, highlighting limitations in their ability to integrate global contextual information with local observations. SituRecBench provides a rigorous foundation for advancing the development of situated assistants in the real world.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10349) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10349)

## 232. Guided Co-Attention with Adaptive Gating for Multimodal Medical Data Fusion

- **作者**：Muntaqim Ahmed Raju, Priyanka Siddappa, Md Shifat Haider Al Amin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：IEEE BIBM 2026

Multimodal fusion requires not only exchanging information across modalities but also determining which representations should participate in the interaction and how complementary cross-modal views should be combined. We introduce Guided Co-Attention with Adaptive Gating, a fusion framework that integrates token-wise guidance, bidirectional cross-attention, and sample-specific fusion. The proposed method constructs two directional representations: an image-conditioned text representation, obtained by allowing textual tokens to query visual features, and a text-conditioned image representation formed in the reverse direction. Token-wise guidance modulates the representations before cross-attention, while an adaptive gate learns the relative contribution of the two resulting directional representations for each sample. We evaluate the approach using paired chest radiographs and radiology reports as an image-text test bed for binary (normal vs. abnormal) classification. The two cross-attention directions exhibit distinct performance characteristics, demonstrating that reversing the query and source modalities does not produce interchangeable representations. Ablation experiments further show that bidirectional interaction, adaptive gating, and token guidance provide complementary benefits. The proposed model achieves 96.05% accuracy, 96.10% F1-score, 98.37% AUC, 95.60% Sens@95Spec, and 96.51% Spec@95Sens, yielding the highest mean performance across all evaluated metrics. These findings support explicitly modeling interaction direction, token relevance, and sample-dependent integration when constructing multimodal representations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10348) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10348)

## 233. PoseBench: Probing Pose Awareness in Visual Foundation Models

- **作者**：Most Nilufa Yeasmin, Draix Wyatt, Ruyi Lian
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Pose awareness characterizes the extent to which a learned feature space aligns with the underlying object-pose manifold, such that observations with similar poses exhibit greater feature similarity than those with increasingly different poses. While such structure is desirable for pose-sensitive 3D vision tasks, visual foundation models pre-trained on individual 2D images typically lack explicit 3D or pose supervision. This raises a fundamental question: Do 2D visual foundation models exhibit pose awareness after large-scale pretraining? To answer this question, we introduce PoseBench, a systematic benchmark to probe pose awareness in pretrained visual features. Our evaluation follows a progressive design: held-out rendered views probe intrinsic pose-aware structure under controlled conditions, while real-image queries evaluate how robustly this structure remains accessible under real-world variations. We further introduce pose-graph-based refinement as a complementary probe of compatibility between feature neighborhoods and the underlying pose manifold. Across 31 publicly available checkpoints from 10 pretraining families, PoseBench reveals that pose awareness is an implicit but non-uniform property of 2D visual foundation models, and consistent gains from pose-graph refinement suggest that feature spaces retain structure compatible with the underlying pose manifold. PoseBench therefore provides a systematic basis for understanding and selecting pretrained features for pose-sensitive 3D vision tasks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10347) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10347)

## 234. MaRiO: Multi-agent Collaborative Reasoning via Shared Observations in MLLMs

- **作者**：Nathaniel Redmond, Fidel Omar Tito Cruz, Devansh Sharma et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to the NeurIPS 2026 Evaluations & Datasets Track (poster). First two authors contributed equally

Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) show strong visual perception capabilities, but existing evaluations largely focus on single-view or multi-view settings with shared camera parameters, leaving the challenge of multi-agent collaborative scenarios underexplored. In such settings, multiple agents observe a shared environment from independent viewpoints, and reliable decisions require reasoning across these independent observations of the same environment. To study this problem, we introduce MaRiO, a benchmark for evaluating multi-agent collaborative reasoning, and evaluate 23 state-of-the-art MLLMs on it. Our analysis reveals three key findings: (1) tasks governed by static perception are generally easier than tasks that require transformational event sequences; (2) most models struggle to resolve cross-agent correspondences, with errors increasing with scene complexity; and (3) models show limited ability to leverage geometric cues present in the scene. As an initial step toward addressing these challenges, we explore geometry-guided synthetic view augmentation that generates intermediate transition frames between agent observations, providing auxiliary spatial context that improves performance in certain categories. Overall, our results highlight that multi-agent collaborative reasoning remains an open challenge and an important direction for multimodal embodied systems. The code is available on the project page: https://omarftt.github.io/MaRiO-page/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10344) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10344)

## 235. On the universal structure in deterministic generative diffusion

- **作者**：Defang Chen, Zhenyu Zhou, Can Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cond-mat.stat-mech, cs.CV, stat.ML

Diffusion-based generative modeling has emerged as a general computational framework for simulating high-dimensional, structured data across various domains. Understanding its generative mechanisms is paramount for advancing model training and inference in a principled way. Here, we demonstrate that deterministic sampling trajectories share a universal geometric structure invariant to data modalities and diffusion model implementations. This structure can be largely explained by an exactly solvable model based on the optimal linear denoiser.

## 236. TimeTraveler: Temporal Strategy Planning with Time Dictionary for Streaming Video Understanding

- **作者**：Minkuk Kim, Junho Kim, Seong Tae Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://geppa.github.io/TimeTraveler

Streaming video understanding (SVU) requires models to answer user queries over continuously evolving video streams. Unlike offline video QA, SVU must handle queries whose evidence may lie in the past, appear in the current scene, or emerge only after future observations. Existing streaming video systems often rely on compact visual memories or fixed evidence-access schemes, which can lose fine-grained details and fail to adapt the answering process to the temporal intent of each query. In this paper, we propose TimeTraveler, a streaming VQA framework that performs temporal strategy planning with a Time Dictionary. Inspired by the need to search across different temporal regions of a stream, TimeTraveler stores each observed moment as a timestamp-indexed structured caption and uses this dictionary as an explicit source of queryable evidence. Given a query, TimeTraveler determines whether to recall past evidence, read the present context, or wait for future observations, and then applies a strategy-specific evidence acquisition process. Comparative experiments on streaming video QA benchmarks show that TimeTraveler improves streaming question answering by preserving fine-grained temporal evidence and selecting the appropriate temporal strategy for each query. The project page is available at https://geppa.github.io/TimeTraveler.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10337) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10337)

## 237. Evaluating classification and explainability in multimodal petrographic image analysis

- **作者**：Thomas James Wood, Oktay Karakuş, Can Eyüpoğlu
- **投稿**：2026-11-07 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

Automated thin-section classifiers increasingly combine plane-polarised and cross-polarised light (PPL and XPL), two complementary views central to petrographic identification. Classification accuracy alone does not reveal whether model errors reflect mineralogical relationships, how predictions depend on each optical view, or whether attribution maps identify regions that influence model decisions. Here, we examine these complementary aspects of the released LITHOS dual-stream vision transformer using outputs from an earlier evaluation of 105,802 expert-annotated grains across 25 categories. Classification errors frequently followed the label taxonomy: 72.6\% of misclassified plagioclase, microcline and sanidine grains were assigned the generic Feldspar label, while 87.1\% of quartz-family grains received a label within that family, compared with 66.6\% assigned their exact label. Replacing the XPL input with its per-channel spatial means produced a greater reduction in true-class probability than equivalent PPL replacement in 20 of 25 categories, but not in the three quartz categories. Five explainable artificial intelligence (XAI) attribution procedures generated visually distinct maps for identical inputs. In a superpixel-deletion analysis involving 30 grains from 29 thin sections, method-minus-random contrasts ranged from $-0.043$ to $0.057$, with all 41 simultaneous 95\% confidence intervals including zero. Together, these findings show that error structure, modality sensitivity and attribution behaviour capture distinct aspects of model performance that cannot be established from classification accuracy or visual inspection of attribution maps alone.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10331) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10331)

## 238. LoCo: Selective Local Competition for Discriminative Open-Vocabulary Multi-Label Recognition

- **作者**：Zhuoming Li, Bo Han, Xiaoyu Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：The paper is accepted by NeurIPS 2026

Open-Vocabulary Multi-Label Recognition (OV-MLR) aims to identify all queried semantic concepts present in an image. Existing methods mainly improve global image-text matching or text-side representations, leaving local prediction largely under-explored. In this paper, we revisit local prediction and reveal that it can serve as a strong discriminative branch once two properties are properly handled: spatial consistency of local visual features and compatibility-aware local competition. This perspective is motivated by local semantic sparsity: each image region is related to only a small subset of queried concepts, while compatible concepts of different granularities or types may still coexist locally. We instantiate this perspective with LoCo, a selective Local Competition framework for OV-MLR. LoCo adopts a spatially consistent visual encoder to reduce local feature entanglement, and introduces Selective Softmax to impose competition only among locally incompatible categories while preserving compatible responses. Multi-scale inference further improves local prediction at different scales. LoCo can also be ensembled with an existing global predictor to provide complementary image-level context. Experiments on four multi-label benchmarks show that standalone LoCo improves average F1-score and mAP over TagCLIP by 3.4% and 3.8%, respectively. LoCo + DualPrompt achieves the highest averages among the compared methods, with gains of 4.8% and 5.2% over TagCLIP. Our results suggest that local semantic sparsity offers a useful basis for developing more discriminative open-vocabulary multi-label recognition methods. The code is available at https://github.com/lee-plus-plus/LoCo.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10321) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10321)

## 239. AI-Driven Repair of Missing Automotive Components

- **作者**：Kazim Abbas, Zheng Liu
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：6 pages, 4 figures

Due to supply constraints, replacing damaged automotive components can be expensive and time-consuming, while replacing the entire component can be wasteful when much of it remains usable. This paper presents a pipeline to investigate whether missing geometry can be reconstructed as a repair patch using paired observations of the intact models and synthetically damaged components. Noise-free color, depth, and silhouette images were produced using the camera viewpoints. Depth differences were then used to identify the potential missing regions, while multi-view voxel reconstruction was used to estimate the missing portion. A pretrained DINOv2 model was also added to validate missing candidates and determine whether pretrained visual features could improve reconstruction. We tested the pipeline on 20 automotive CAD models, each with a synthetically removed portion. Each model was tested under four different conditions using two reconstruction methods for a total of 160 trials. The predictions were compared with the missing geometry of the unchanged ground-truth using predefined thresholds of the voxel intersection-over-union over 0.85 and the absolute relative volume error less than 15%. Of the 160 experimental trials, 109 of them satisfied both thresholds. Geometry-only reconstruction passed 16 of 20 components with eight views but only 10 of 20 with four adjacent views. Further analysis showed that 24 out of the 37 failed reconstructions could be categorized as unsuccessful because extra material was incorrectly added to areas that should have been empty. The implementation of DINOv2 into the pipeline did not improve the overall reconstruction accuracy and made prediction generation slower. For 13 verified tests, printing only the repaired sections instead of the entire parts reduced filament use by 93.08% and printing time by 89.39% based on the median results.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10318) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10318)

## 240. Uncertainty, Not Concealment: A Mechanistic Reappraisal of Sycophancy in Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Mohamed I. Eldagla, Zeyad H. ElShafei, Ahmed M. Anter
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted to the NeurIPS 2026 Med-Reasoner Workshop

Vision-language models (VLMs) in medical retrieval-augmented generation must be able to say when a retrieved report contradicts the X-ray, so understanding whether they can detect such conflicts is important for safe deployment. The sycophancy mask hypothesis holds that these models internally detect conflicts but refuse to report them, concealing a detected contradiction. We introduce a three-part diagnostic that separates concealment from uncertainty: representation probing, activation steering, and two-way forced choice. Across four instruction-tuned VLMs on chest X-rays, we confirm behavioral suppression of contradiction and find that a linear probe separates matched from mismatched inputs, beyond what a simple CLIP model achieves. Steering the learned conflict direction never elicits contradiction, and forcing a binary choice does not recover one; instead, cross-modal attention to the image drops on mismatched pairs. We conclude that these models fail to contradict because the mismatch registers as hedged, gated uncertainty rather than concealed knowledge, calling for re-grounding and uncertainty surfacing. Code is available at https://github.com/mohamed-eldagla/Uncertainty-Not-Concealment/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10297) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10297)

## 241. DAC-MACE: Context-Conditioned Expert Routing for Compact Scene Coordinate Regression in Camera Relocalization

- **作者**：Dingying Fan, Mingkai Liu, Haohua Que et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 6 figures. Submitted to IEEE Sensors Journal

A camera becomes an absolute pose sensor when its images can be relocalized against a prebuilt map of the scene. On mobile robots and augmented-reality devices, that map must be small and quick to build. Scene coordinate regression (SCR) stores the scene in a compact network that predicts a 3D coordinate for every pixel, and mixture-of-experts (MoE) variants extend its capacity to larger outdoor scenes. In existing MoE-based SCR, however, the router assigns each pixel to an expert from the same local descriptor that the experts regress from, so repeated facades and windows at different locations receive similar routing inputs. We present DAC-MACE, which conditions soft expert routing on recalibrated and contextualized features. A residual context adapter (RCA) reweights the channels and locations of frozen encoder features, a global context encoder (GCE) mixes them with multi-scale self-attention, and an image-level pooled descriptor is added to the router input. A dual-threshold RANSAC--Levenberg--Marquardt estimator recovers the pose. On Cambridge Landmarks, DAC-MACE attains a mean per-scene median translation error of 13.0 cm over three seeds, the lowest among the RGB-only SCR methods compared (GLACE 13.6 cm, MACE 14.4 cm), and reduces the error on the largest scene from 24 to 19 cm relative to MACE. In a cumulative study, the MoE head, GCE, RCA, and the dual-threshold estimator lower the mean error from 17.0 cm to 14.4, 14.0, 13.6, and 13.0 cm. On 7-Scenes, adding GCE to a single ACE head raises Recall@(5 cm, 5$^\circ$) from 86.56% to 87.60%.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10263) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10263)

## 242. Domain Generalization for Style-Debiased Object Detection via Label-Consistent Training across Multiple Styles

- **作者**：Chaoran Zhang, Shirshak Bhusal, Zhilin Gao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Domain generalization (DG) for object detection aims to improve model performance on unseen target domains. Existing detectors, however, suffer substantial degradation on visually distinct unseen domains, as models confined to limited training styles tend to overfit superficial statistical cues rather than object-level semantics. Motivated by human perception, which attends primarily to semantically relevant features while ignoring irrelevant style cues, we propose Origin-Anchored Multi-Style Consistency (OAMSC), a source-only framework that couples multi-style data synthesis with cross-style consistency learning to suppress style-sensitive shortcut learning. For data preparation, we leverage off-the-shelf diffusion models to expand the source datasets into 20 distinct styles, with all generated images manually verified to ensure that object instances and annotations remain consistent across style variants. During training, we impose Origin-anchored consistency constraints at scene and object levels to reinforce the learning of domain-stable features while suppressing the influence of style-sensitive cues. Extensive experiments and ablation studies on multiple challenging DG benchmarks demonstrate the consistent effectiveness and strong generalization performance of OAMSC across diverse domain shifts. Notably, our approach applies to both one-stage and two-stage detectors, while its data-centric design is complementary in principle to other DG methods. The training code and two style-transfer datasets constructed for the experiments, comprising approximately 180K images in total, are publicly available on GitHub.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10262) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10262)

## 243. STAIRS: Staging the Inverse Problem via Admissible-Support Scheduling for Unrolled MRI Reconstruction

- **作者**：Xiangjian Hou, Chang Ni, Chao Qin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Accelerated magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) forms images from incomplete Fourier measurements, shifting acquisition burden to computational reconstruction. Deep unrolled networks interleave learned regularization and data consistency, yet typically estimate the full unmeasured spectrum at every cascade. We propose STAIRS (Staging the Inverse Problem via Admissible-Support Scheduling), which projects intermediate reconstructions onto nested Fourier supports that expand to the full spectrum and conditions each update on its support transition. Conditional increments of a regularized log-determinant score of training covariance define a normalized spectral coordinate, accounting for dependencies between newly and previously admitted frequencies. Dyadic targets allocate larger coordinate increments to earlier cascades and are mapped to fixed supports before training, enabling coarse-to-fine reconstruction with unchanged sampling masks and data consistency. On fastMRI and Stanford2D, STAIRS improves matched Transformer (HUMUSNet) and convolutional (NAFNet) backbones in single- and multi-coil reconstruction, including radiologist-annotated pathology regions. It also outperforms predictor-based progression with lower parameter overhead and inference latency. Stage-wise analysis shows repeated error correction by projection and lower intermediate reconstruction errors than the backbones alone throughout support expansion. Ablations favor dyadic allocation over uniform expansion for square and line-band supports and over manual front-loading for square windows. Support projection alone improves reconstruction, and transition conditioning strengthens the gain. Cross-dataset evaluation shows improvements for both backbones in both directions. Support allocation thus complements regularizer design in unrolled MRI reconstruction.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10260) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10260)

## 244. Beyond Frame-Level Accuracy: Analyzing Video-Level Stability in Transformer-Based Deepfake Forensics

- **作者**：Syed Azmul Hasan, Mowmita Pervin Hera, Afroza Lona Mim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：Paper submitted to international journal of intelligent systems, Wiley publishers, accepted version of open-access paper

Deepfake videos generated by advanced generative models pose serious threats to digital security, misinformation control, and media forensics. Although numerous detection methods have been proposed, their reported performances are often based on heterogeneous preprocessing pipelines, inconsistent data splits, and model-specific training protocols, hindering fair architectural comparison. This work presents a controlled and reproducible comparative study of convolutional neural networks (ResNet-18 and EfficientNet-B0) and a transformer-based architecture (ViT-B/16) within a unified, face-centric deepfake detection framework. All models are trained using identical preprocessing, augmentation, optimization settings, and data splits on Celeb-DF v2 and FaceForensics++, and results are validated across three independent random seeds with bootstrapped confidence intervals. Experimental results show that while CNN-based models perform competitively at the frame level, their performance degrades after video-level aggregation. In contrast, ViT-B/16 demonstrates more stable video-level performance, achieving 92.8\% [95\% CI: 91.7--94.0] video-level accuracy and an AUC of 0.950 [95\% CI: 0.934--0.964] on Celeb-DF v2 (3-seed bootstrapped). Cross-dataset evaluation across the same three random seeds reveals a large, asymmetric generalization gap (AUC 0.79 FF++$\rightarrow$Celeb-DF vs. 0.64 Celeb-DF$\rightarrow$FF++), indicating that in-domain video-level stability does not transfer to unseen manipulation domains. These findings indicate that global self-attention improves robustness and video-level stability for deepfake detection within a given data domain, while highlighting the need for further work on cross-domain generalization.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10249) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10249)

## 245. From Weapons to Events: Evaluating Evidential Support in Multimodal AI for Policing

- **作者**：Armin Mustafa, Syed Sameed Husain
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CY
- **备注**：34 pages, 2 figures, 4 tables; appendices include annotation guidelines, prompt templates and a datasheet

Computer vision (CV) for policing has centred on recognising objects such as seized weapons, while body-worn video (BWV) is still reviewed by people, with existing AI tools using only its audio. Multimodal video AI could make BWV review faster and cheaper. At the object level, we present the KnifeHunter dataset, developed with UK law enforcement, with over 25,000 images of 543 knife classes and a protocol reflecting investigative conditions, and KHNet, a retrieval architecture fusing local and global features, which with about 125,000 distractor images attains 85.7% and 63.4% mean Average Precision (mAP) on the medium and hard protocols. At the event level, we present an evaluation protocol for video understanding pipelines, which describe a video as events and a transcript, that scores transcription, speaker attribution, event detection, temporal localisation and unsupported events. Whether each event is supported by the recording is labelled by a judge validated against blinded human labels. We apply it to complete pipelines, three 2026 omni-modal models and our hybrid NarrativeBridge-PAL-GPT-5 pipeline, on UKBWV-27, a pilot stress-test set of 27 publicly released UK BWV recordings (118 minutes). On this set the hybrid pipeline leads on five of six measures, yet it leaves 13.6% of predicted events unsupported and misattributes 27.7% of utterances, and every pipeline degrades on recordings from edited releases, long recordings and noisy audio. We propose readiness levels separating assistive tasks from those requiring verification of every output.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10245) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10245)

## 246. OfficeTwin: Realizing 3D Reconstruction of Redundant Scenes through Partial Viewpoint Perception

- **作者**：Quanyun Wu, Kyle Gao, Daisy Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Feed-forward 3D reconstruction turns an uncalibrated monocular stream into a dense point cloud in a single inference pass, orders of magnitude faster than classical structure-from-motion (e.g., COLMAP), and reliable on the weakly textured, repetitive surfaces where keypoint matching fails outright. However, raw point clouds lack the object identity, physical properties, and watertight volume required for robot learning. This gap widens under robot-native capture. A camera mounted on a robot constrained to a few degrees of freedom sees each object only partially, and the repetitive, weakly textured structure of an office makes distinct workstations nearly indistinguishable, leaving redundant and partial observations that downstream reconstruction cannot resolve. Existing image-to-3D generation methods struggle with these conditions, hallucinating geometry under incomplete views and confusing repeated instances. We present OfficeTwin, a robot-native framework that converts these incomplete observations into simulation-ready digital twins. OfficeTwin first imposes metric and structural constraints, then exploits the office's structural redundancy by registering repeated cubicles into a shared canonical frame, where complementary observations recover geometry occluded in any single view without hallucination. On a real office scan, OfficeTwin recovers every cubicle as an individually watertight, metric solid, outperforming feed-forward reconstruction and generative baselines that fail to produce simulation-ready assets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10241) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10241)

## 247. Anatomy-Preserving Unpaired Medical Image Translation via Shared Latent Anchoring

- **作者**：Zhilin Zou, Hieu Le, Saumya Gupta et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026

Unpaired medical image-to-image translation requires synthesizing target-modality images from source-modality images without aligned supervision. Existing methods learn a direct source-to-target mapping that entangles structure preservation and appearance generation within a single model, often distorting clinically meaningful anatomy in the translated output. We argue that these two objectives should be decoupled entirely: anatomical structure should be captured independently of generation, and the generator should operate only on a frozen structural representation. To this end, we propose a two-stage framework for unpaired medical image translation. In the first stage, we learn a shared structure space by training a structure encoder-decoder on source images, target images, and structural masks jointly, using mask reconstruction to anchor the latent space to modality-agnostic anatomy. For source images without mask annotations, a target-domain memory bank provides soft structural supervision via prototype retrieval. In the second stage, a conditional flow model learns to render target-domain appearance under fixed structural conditions. This ensures the generator inherits target appearance statistics and can focus entirely on structural conditioning rather than learning appearance from scratch. We validate our framework on OCT-to-OCTA synthesis and brain MR-to-CT translation. Our method outperforms all unpaired baselines on both tasks and, despite requiring no paired supervision, also surpasses paired baselines, improving PSNR by up to 11.2% and SSIM by up to 26.4% on OCT-to-OCTA, and achieving consistent gains across whole-image, soft-tissue, and bone regions on MR-to-CT. The project repository is available on GitHub.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10236) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10236)

## 248. FAIR Universe Weak Lensing ML Uncertainty Challenge: Dataset and Cosmological Inference Competition

- **作者**：Biwei Dai, Po-Wen Chang, Erwan Allys et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：astro-ph.CO, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG, physics.data-an
- **备注**：10+7 pages, 2+4 figures, 2+1 tables. Key results of the NeurIPS 2025 FAIR Universe Weak Lensing ML Challenge Phase 1. The challenge dataset is publicly available at Zenodo: https://zenodo.org/records/20056065. Challenge GitHub repository: https://github.com/FAIR-Universe/Cosmology_Challenge

Weak gravitational lensing is a powerful cosmological probe that enables direct mapping of the matter distribution and precise constraints on the fundamental parameters governing structure formation in the universe. In recent years, machine learning has shown strong potential for extracting cosmological information from weak-lensing data beyond traditional summary statistics. However, progress has been limited by the lack of standardized benchmarks that capture the complexity of the dataset, simulator imperfections, and various systematic biases encountered in realistic analyses. To address this gap, we present the FAIR Universe Weak Lensing Machine Learning Uncertainty Challenge, one of the NeurIPS 2025 competitions, as a benchmark dataset and evaluation study. Our dataset reflects realistic weak-lensing observations, incorporating multiple systematic biases, and thereby providing a controlled testbed for robust cosmological inference under uncertainties. We summarize the main methodological advances developed by the winners of our challenge, including data augmentation with conditional optimal transport, an AI-agent-driven approach for parameter inference, ensemble learning, Vision Transformer-based architectures, and physics-informed feature integration. By unifying the benchmark dataset and the winning solutions, this work provides a community resource for developing and evaluating machine learning methods for weak lensing cosmology and, more broadly, for scientific inference under systematic uncertainties.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10228) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10228)

## 249. RigidGS: Geometry-Aware Object Decoupling for Rigid-Body Interaction in 3D Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Paul Sansah Gyreyiri, Diksha Shukla
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, 5 figures, 7 tables. Accepted at WACV 2027 (Applications Track). Project page: https://gyreyirisansah.github.io/rigidgs

Interactive VR/MR applications demand photorealistic 3D representations that support real-time rendering and object-level manipulation. While 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) achieves high rendering quality, it represents scenes as unstructured, monolithic primitives. Existing object-aware 3DGS methods jointly optimize geometry and semantic states but suffer from two key failure modes: noisy foundation-model masks on complex geometry, and label-geometry coupling, in which co-optimized identity gradients mislabel boundary Gaussians. We introduce RigidGS, an end-to-end pipeline that decouples geometric learning from object identity. We propose a depth- and temporally-aware mask refinement module that addresses two complementary SAM2 failures: over-inclusion on thin structures and under-inclusion on multimodal-depth objects. We then determine object identities entirely upstream via COLMAP projection voting and freeze them during training, directing mask supervision solely toward geometric optimization. Refined masks alone substantially improve competing methods, yet a residual gap in label-geometry coupling persists that our fixed-identity design eliminates. RigidGS outperforms prior object-aware 3DGS methods on LLFF/NVOS, Mip-NeRF~360, and iNeRF360, improving segmentation and boundary IoU by 7.7 and 14.5 points on LLFF/NVOS. Finally, RigidGS enables real-time rigid-body manipulation on Meta Quest~2 without mesh extraction. Our code and project page are available at https://gyreyirisansah.github.io/rigidgs.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10199) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10199)

## 250. ATLAS: A Multi-Procedure Benchmark and Context-Aware Model for Surgical Anatomy Segmentation

- **作者**：Ronald L. P. D. de Jong, Tim J. M. Jaspers, Raf A. H. Vervoort et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recognizing anatomical structures during minimally invasive surgery (MIS) could support intraoperative navigation and improve patient safety, but progress is limited by small, single-procedure datasets and by segmentation methods developed for natural scenes. This work addresses both limitations through a new dataset and model. First, we introduce ATLAS-120k, a clip-level semantic segmentation dataset of 121,018 annotated frames from 100 surgical videos, covering 14 laparoscopic and robot-assisted procedures and 41 classes. Annotations were created with a pipeline that combines manual labeling, tracker-based propagation, and review by surgeons. Second, we propose ATLAS (Anatomy Recognition with Context Learning using Foundation Representations), a video semantic segmentation model for surgical anatomy recognition. ATLAS builds on in-domain foundation-model embeddings and adds context queries for the procedure and surgical phase, together with query propagation between frames for short-term memory. ATLAS outperforms state-of-the-art natural-image and in-domain foundation models in both segmentation accuracy and temporal consistency, achieving a Dice score of 0.70 with ViT-L while running in real time at 76.2 FPS. Ablations show that the context queries improve accuracy and that query propagation improves temporal consistency. ATLAS also generalizes well, matching a dedicated model zero-shot on CholecSeg8k and improving phase recognition on AutoLaparo over SurgeNet initialization. The model weights, dataset annotations, and annotation tool are publicly available at https://github.com/TimJaspers0801/ATLAS.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10182) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10182)

## 251. CXR-CMFA: A Cross-Modal Feature Aggregation Network for Chest X-Ray Pathology Classification

- **作者**：Talukder Hasnat Zadid, Jahin Sultana, Felipe Bochini Brisola et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Chest X-ray (CXR) interpretation remains challenging because subtle pathology can be obscured by overlapping anatomy, image-quality variation, and limited visual evidence in isolated radiographs. Although image-only deep learning models provide useful baselines, they discard the complementary diagnostic context available in radiology reports, while large vision-language models can be computationally expensive for practical deployment. This paper presents CXR-CMFA, a lightweight cross-modal feature aggregation network for binary CXR pathology classification. The proposed framework combines a dual-stream visual encoder, using EfficientNet-B0 for global anatomical representation and DenseNet-121 for local pathological detail, with a TF-IDF-MLP report encoder. Two fusion modules integrate these modalities: the Dual-Modal Spatial Attention Module (DMSAM) uses the report embedding to gate global and local visual features, and the Cross-Modal Gated Feature Fusion (CGFF) module learns per-instance weights for visual and textual contributions. Experiments on the MIMIC-CXR benchmark, using the provided train, validation, and test splits, show that CXR-CMFA achieves 93.45% accuracy, 99.88% precision, 91.8% recall, 95.7% F1-score, and 99.19% AUC on the held-out test set. The model produces only four false positives among 984 normal test cases, supporting its suitability for high-specificity screening workflows. An ablation study further demonstrates a 22.47 percentage-point AUC improvement over the global-image-only variant, confirming the value of adaptive cross-modal fusion. These results indicate that compact report-conditioned fusion can provide strong discriminative performance without relying on large pretrained language models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10174) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10174)

## 252. Improving the Diffusability of Motion Tokenizer

- **作者**：Guanhe Huang, Songqiao Han, Tangzheng Lian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026 (Spotlight). 26 pages, including appendix

Latent diffusion models paired with a variational auto-encoder (VAE) tokenizer have shown promising performance for efficient text-driven human motion generation. However, motion latent diffusion suffers from a generation-reconstruction trade-off: simply increasing tokenizer capacity enhances reconstruction but fails to proportionally improve text-conditioned generation quality. We assume this trade-off stems from a spectral mismatch inside the VAE tokenizer: the encoder yields a latent space biased toward high frequencies, and the decoder introduces temporal artifacts. In this paper, we propose the Diffusable Motion Tokenizer (DiMoT) to improve the diffusability of the tokenizer, rather than scaling up the diffusion backbone. To improve text-conditioned optimization, Critic Spectral Adaptation (CSA) leverages a pretrained diffusion model as the critic to suppress high-frequency latent energy. To preserve motion dynamics, the Alpha-Flow Motion Decoder (AMD) performs generative decoding via average-velocity prediction, enabling efficient one-step decoding. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DiMoT achieves state-of-the-art performance across various challenging text-to-motion benchmarks, yielding superior motion fidelity and text-motion alignment. Project page: https://githubhgh.github.io/dimot/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10160) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10160)

## 253. BATCH: Behavior Alignment for Cross-Embodiment Learning from Humans

- **作者**：Zeyi Liu, Shuran Song
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project website: https://batch-embodiment.github.io/

Human demonstrations provide a scalable source of diverse manipulation data, but leveraging them for robot learning remains challenging due to differences in embodiment, viewpoint, action spaces, and temporal structure. We introduce BATCH, a cross-embodiment learning framework that explicitly aligns human and robot behaviors. BATCH learns self-supervised temporal representations with a cycle-consistency objective to establish cross-embodiment correspondences without manual annotation. We further introduce two complementary training objectives that leverage these correspondences within an autoregressive videoaction diffusion model: behavior-aligned co-training and human-to-robot style transfer. On real-world manipulation tasks, BATCH improves generalization to unseen task configurations compared with training on unaligned data and prior cross-embodiment baselines, with additional gains from the style-transfer objective. Our results demonstrate that explicit behavior alignment enables more effective transfer from human demonstrations to robot policies.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10148) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10148)

## 254. Dataset Collections: Challenges of Large-Scale Data Aggregation in 3D Medical Image Datasets

- **作者**：Yannick Kirchhoff, Saikat Roy, Elisa Stegmeier et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 E&D Track

The development of deep learning-based medical AI depends on high-quality annotated data. Yet, dataset creation remains constrained by the need for expert radiologist annotations, limiting curated datasets to relatively small cohorts. To overcome this limitation, recent efforts increasingly aggregate disparate public datasets into large-scale dataset collections. However, combining datasets at scale often obscures data provenance, introducing substantial risks of reuse propagation, unintended leakage, bias amplification and distorted data distributions. In this work, we study these problems on a massive collection of 565k 3D radiological images across 1034 public datasets and provide practical resources to address them. Firstly, we formalize dataset collections as a distinct paradigm in medical image analysis and characterize their structural challenges, including reuse propagation, duplication, and provenance fragmentation. Secondly, we perform a large-scale empirical study of duplication on this collection and demonstrate extensive exact and near-duplicate reuse across public datasets via perceptual hashing and manual tracing, identifying a lower bound of 118k duplicate pairs. We make the perceptual hashes publicly accessible for comparison through our website, providing researchers with a standardized reference for identifying potential overlaps and shared provenance. Thirdly, since hashing alone cannot detect all duplicates, we introduce MAP (Metadata for Aggregation and Provenance), a lightweight, portable metadata profile for documenting provenance, known overlaps, and deduplication decisions in dataset collections. Our contributions help dataset collections shift from coarse aggregations to reliable and provenance-aware data scaling mechanisms in medical image analysis. A web tool supporting our work is available at https://mic-dct.dkfz.de/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10144) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10144)

## 255. Beyond Prompt Attacks: Least-Action Auditing of Concept Erasure

- **作者**：Yifei Zhu
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CR, cs.CV
- **备注**：22 pages, 12 figures

Concept erasure, or concept unlearning, in text-to-image diffusion models is usually audited with prompt attacks. A prompt attack tests whether one conditioning string still retrieves the erased concept. It does not measure the probability that the frozen generator still assigns to that concept. We introduce least-action auditing of this residual probability, and evaluate it on Stable Diffusion concept-erasure checkpoints. The least-action barrier is the minimum control cost of driving the frozen reverse process into a detector-defined concept event. This cost equals the path-space KL divergence of conditioning the model on the event, and therefore the event's negative log-probability. The estimate $ΔS_{\mathrm{rob}}$ uses a CLIP$\wedge$DINOv2 consensus terminal, with no retraining and no adversarial prompt. Across $18$ erased concept-mechanism pairs spanning object classes and NSFW nudity, $ΔS_{\mathrm{rob}}$ predicts deployment leakage counted on $1{,}500$ fresh samples per model by detectors excluded from the audit (pooled Spearman $ρ{=}{-}0.76$, $p{=}5{\times}10^{-4}$). Within every audited concept, the eraser with the higher barrier leaks less ($18/18$). On NSFW nudity the barrier orders the standard erasers exactly: UCE $ΔS_{\mathrm{rob}}{=}3.9$ (leak $16.4\%$) $<$ ESD $75.5$ ($9.8\%$) $<$ RECE $224.5$ ($1.4\%$), while Safe Latent Diffusion (SLD) stays near zero and leaks $85.5\%$ under explicit prompts. The same trajectories repair deployed checkpoints. An audit-guided closed-form patch reduces UCE nudity leakage from $16.2\%$ to $0/1{,}500$ and RECE church leakage by $3.3\times$, where direction-count-matched random edits fail.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10106) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10106)

## 256. A Privacy-Aware Deep Learning Benchmark for Facial Emotion Recognition

- **作者**：Larissa Palhares, Maria Machado, Hudson Bruno et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：7 pages, 2 figures

Facial Expression Recognition (FER) traditionally relies on raw RGB images, introducing privacy risks, sensitivity to illumination variations, and susceptibility to identity-confounding visual shortcuts. As an alternative, this work proposes a lightweight, privacy-aware pipeline using exclusively geometric representations-facial landmarks (478 points) and blendshapes (52 coefficients, ARKit standard)-eliminating persistent raw video storage. Six neural architectures trained from scratch on fused CREMA-D and RAVDESS datasets were benchmarked against an ImageNet-pretrained ResNet50 baseline under a 5-fold subject-independent cross-validation protocol. Geometric models consistently outperformed the pixel baseline on unseen subjects with 12-124 times fewer parameters, while demonstrating superior robustness on subtle emotional expressions such as sadness. Ablation studies reveal that blendshapes capture the primary discriminative signal, while landmarks provide essential complementary spatial topology. These results demonstrate that compact geometric representations preserve affective semantics, eliminate downstream transfer learning dependency on visual backbones, and enable efficient, privacy-aware emotion recognition.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10097) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10097)

## 257. Contrastive fine-tuning against stain and scanner bias improves robustness and downstream performance across pathology foundation models

- **作者**：Ryan Kim, Hassan Muhammad, Anish Dulal et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：ASCI Workshop, Neurips 2026

Histopathology foundation models achieve strong performance across diverse downstream tasks, yet their representations have been shown to encode scanner- and site-specific biases, creating generalization gaps and potentially limiting real-world utility. We introduce SPECTRA (Scanner and Pathology Embedding Contrastive Tuning for Robust Alignment), a model-agnostic contrastive learning post-training framework which explicitly encourages scanner- and stain-robust representations, to optimize performance and generalization on downstream applications. Using parameter-efficient LoRA adapters trained on matched multi-scanner and multi-stain whole-slide images sourced from the PLISM dataset, our method aligns spatially corresponding regions across acquisition conditions (scanner, stain), using other locations within each candidate condition as negatives. SPECTRA improved PathoROB robustness index, gene-expression prediction on HEST, slide-level classification AUC on CPTAC, and held-out PLISM retrieval for every model. Overall tile-level classification on THUNDER also improved, with exceptions for segmentation and calibration tasks. We open-source our training and evaluation codebases and release adapters and model weights for selected backbones.

## 258. Adapting Frozen Promptable Segmenters to User-Defined Taxonomies via Few-Shot Proposal Learning

- **作者**：Khayrul Islam, Christopher Frederic Miller
- **投稿**：2026-11-06 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：LLNL-CONF-2026172

Promptable segmenters of the SAM family turn a click or a phrase into an instance mask, so the costly part of a label is now its class. However, each annotation project draws its own class boundary, often through objects these models already know: people from cars, sedans from trucks, one sedan model from another. A generic model's boundary is fixed by its training vocabulary. We present SNAP, an annotation pipeline that learns the project's boundary from the labels the annotator draws anyway. On one frozen SAM2.1 pass per image, we fit a per-project head, SnapHead, that classifies every proposal and refits in seconds after each labeled image. Where SAM 3 is available, the same labels decide class by class whether to keep its boundary or ours. Given the true masks of eight look-alike cell lines, SAM 3 assigns classes at Cohen's kappa = 0.038 and SnapHead at 0.754 (60 labeled images), and when only the class names change, SAM 3 falls to chance and SnapHead's score is unchanged. End to end, with 20 labeled images, SNAP beats a SAM 3 oracle tuned on the evaluation labels on five microscopy projects (0.484 against 0.441 F1), and run unchanged on five held-out datasets, it beats SAM 3 as shipped on four. Our results suggest that frozen foundation features already encode the class boundaries a generic vocabulary misses, and that a project's own labels are enough to read them out.
